# 5. Apps and tools

## Choosing and Managing Apps: Principles, Budget and Speed

### Principles for choosing apps

- Use an app only for a feature that would be too hard to code yourself. Build simple features with on-site code, because fewer apps usually mean faster pages. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0853.089]
- Before installing an app, check whether it solves a concrete need at your current stage. Apps added because others use them or because they look new usually add cost and load without benefit. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1298.001]
- Install only the apps you need at your current stage. Add more as the business grows and your market calls for specific functions. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.003]
- Think of apps like smartphone apps: you add one for a specific job. [L1300.002]
- Shopify apps are add-on software built by outside developers and listed in the Shopify App Store. Each app adds one function when it is needed, so the store owner does not build that function from scratch. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.001]
- For a store with a limited budget, an app is usually cheaper than custom development. A feature such as live chat built from scratch can cost thousands of dollars and is often less polished than a finished app from a specialist company. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.002]
- Installing an app usually replaces custom development. Features that once required a developer paid thousands of dollars can often be added with a few clicks and no coding. [L1301.010]
- Shopify's built-in features cover most of what a new store needs, so only a few apps are required at the start. Installing extra apps before you need them adds cost and complexity. [L1250.020]
- Shopify is the only paid software that is a must-have at the start. Canva's free version is fine, and Stripe Atlas can help set up a US company if you run the business from abroad. [L0934.054]
- No apps are required at the start of the setup. Settings > Apps lists installed apps. [L0096.015]
- The Shopify App Store offers thousands of apps that extend store functionality beyond the built-in features, such as marketing and SEO tools. [L1313.006]
- When you consider switching a tool, compare the new tool's functions with what your current tool does for you. If the new tool covers everything at lower combined cost, consolidate. If the old tool does a job the new one cannot, keep it. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L0949.024]

### Deciding which apps to install at each stage

#### Setup and foundation

- When setting up a new Shopify store, do not install apps yet unless the current setup step calls for them. Install each app when the stage of the build that needs it arrives. This keeps the store lean and avoids paying for unused subscriptions. [L1301.015]
- At the foundation stage, install only the apps the store needs for its current products and sales. Do not install all apps at once. [L1327.043]
- Fulfillment-agent apps, for example for a 3PL, are often not installed manually because the agent provides its own integration. [L1327.043]
- Channel apps for Google and Meta are added later, when the store starts advertising on those platforms, not during the initial store build. [L1301.016]
- Review collection apps are also added at a later stage, once the store needs them. [L1301.016]
- Beginners do not need SMS marketing, SMS, email or retargeting apps when starting. Focus first on getting views, sessions and sales, and add them later once traction exists. [L0820.034]
- Install only the Ultimate Sales Boost app and one fulfillment app (such as DSers, CJ Dropshipping or an all-in-one fulfillment app) on a new store at the start. [L0820.035]

#### Starting app lists in the sources

The sources give different starting lists. The units do not state a store type or stage for each list, so treat them as alternatives, not as one combined list. Choose by the rule above: install only what the current stage needs. [L1298.001, L1300.003]

| Source | Apps named at start |
|---|---|
| Source C (setup order) | 7 apps, listed in the setup order below |
| Source C (beginner) | Ultimate Sales Boost plus one fulfillment app (DSers, CJ Dropshipping or all-in-one) |
| Source C (essentials) | Loox reviews, Bundle Bear, Ultimate Sales Booster (free version), Image Photo Resizer |
| Source B, 2021-22 (launch core) | Live chat app, review collection app, email marketing autoresponder |
| Source B, 2021-22 (before launch) | TalkTo (chat), Stamp.io (reviews), Drip or Klaviyo (email) |
| Source C (setup table) | Lucky Orange, Tidio, Klaviyo, SMS Bump, Slide cart, CJ Dropshipping, Conversion apps, Quantity breaks, Ryviu |

- The beginner list is the most restrictive. The setup order and the setup table are the broadest. [L0820.035, L1380.036, L1298.004, L1145.025, L0477.001, L1288.008]
- Source B's launch list and the TalkTo, Stamp.io and Drip/Klaviyo list are older advice, from 2021-22. [L1298.004, L1145.025]

#### Source C setup order (as taught)

1. AutoDS: import products, automate fulfillment, pricing rules. [L0477.001]
2. LUX Reviews: import supplier reviews. [L0477.001]
3. Klaviyo: retention email/SMS, signup popup, flows. [L0477.001]
4. SMS Pump: SMS flows. [L0477.001]
5. Tidio Live Chat: chat, chatbots, operating hours. [L0477.001]
6. Bundle Bear: bundles and volume discounts. [L0477.001]
7. BeProfit: profit tracking. [L0477.001]

The setup order is for a dropshipping store and is taught in the course as a sequence. Install each app via the Shopify App Store as described in the installing steps below. [L0477.001, L0477.034]

#### Roles of the core tools

- Klaviyo, SMS Pump and Tidio serve different roles. Klaviyo handles email retention and signup capture. SMS Pump handles SMS abandoned checkout and upsell/win-back. Tidio handles live chat with the visitor during checkout and chatbots for cart recovery and order-status questions. [L0477.033]
- Essential Shopify apps for dropshipping cover four jobs: fulfillment, currency conversion, product reviews, and email/SMS marketing. Optional apps add live chat, a sticky add-to-cart bar, and average order value (AOV) boosters such as bundle or quantity-break apps. [L0635.001]
- Klaviyo is the choice with the deepest Shopify integration. Every extra connector is another point where something can break. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0469.061]
- Source C's setup table lists each app with its setup note and purpose:

| App | Setup note | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| Lucky Orange | Install | Session recordings and heatmaps to see how visitors use the site |
| Tidio | Include automations | Live chat and automated chat replies |
| Klaviyo | Set up abandoned cart and welcome flows | Email and SMS automation |
| SMS Bump | Install | SMS marketing |
| Slide cart | Install | Slide-out cart on the store |
| CJ Dropshipping | Install | Product sourcing and fulfilment link to suppliers |
| Conversion apps | Install | Tools to improve conversion rate |
| Quantity breaks | Install | Volume discounts for larger quantities |
| Ryviu | Install | Product reviews |

[L1288.008]

- Mobile app builders are only worth building when the product has a high repeat purchase rate (e.g. supplements), since push notifications then bring customers back. For normal store products, a mobile app is hard to maintain and gets little traction. [L0472.043]
- Example stack from a 2026 lesson: the store runs Kaching apps for bundles, upsells, cart, popups and subscriptions, plus Judge.me Reviews for reviews, GemPages Builder for page layouts, 17TRACK for tracking and Translate and Adapt for translations. These apps sit beside the native Shopify theme and product template settings. [L1013.016]

#### Currency converters

- A currency converter is required whenever the store does not use Shopify Payments, so customers see prices in their local currency. [L0635.002]
- Shopify Payments already converts currency automatically once a market such as Germany is enabled. [L0635.002]
- Bucks Currency Converter is recommended because it is free. Other currency converter apps perform the same function. [L0635.016]

### Free plan first and budget

#### Rule order

1. Start every new tool on its free plan and check whether it covers the need. [L0337.045]
2. Move to a paid plan only when volume or features force it. When you do, pick the lowest-priced tier that fits the current usage. [L0337.045]
3. Start every app on its free plan, confirm it delivers value, and only then upgrade. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0469.058]
4. Test a paid app during its free trial and measure the extra revenue before paying. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0469.057]

#### Price points and budget limits

- Shopify apps recommended in one 2020 lesson cost less than about $20 per month each. Install them gradually as you reach each step, rather than all at the start. *(older advice, 2020)* [L0358.034]
- A 2021 source said that at about $10 per month, a useful upsell app usually costs little compared with the revenue it can add. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0469.057]
- Easy Tabs - Product Tabs by NexusMedia costs $5 per month with a 7-day free trial. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.007]
- Use both price points as examples only. Check current pricing, because some tools have been rebranded and repriced. [L1283.083]
- Free or low-cost apps with many five-star reviews are an acceptable choice for page speed or image SEO. [L1283.083]
- Shopify apps in the App Store are either free or paid, and the listing page shows which. Apps developed by Shopify are free to add to a store. [L1301.011]
- For a basic automation stack, the free versions of Slack, Trello and Google Sheets are sufficient. There is no need to pay for their team or upgraded plans. Paid upgrades should only be considered when a specific limit is actually reached. [L0437.009]
- Starting ad research: a research tool budget of about $150 per month on ad testing usually gives more experience and data than the tool itself. A paid research tool is more useful once the business is already making money. [L1172.020]
- To begin testing a product you only need a store, an Ad Library reference and a product list. Additional tools are not necessary at the beginning. [L1172.021]
- Ahrefs (SEO research) and SimilarWeb (traffic estimates) are paid tools that are optional for a new dropshipping store. Their discounts, including on Black Friday, do not make them necessary. [L0471.021]
- AI tool costs add up. Each tool in a stack may cost about 10 dollars per month, so a stack of several tools can reach around 100 pounds per month. Justify each tool by the return it gives. [L0353.034]

### Cost traps

- Apps that do not lead to sales should be cancelled, because they add monthly cost without return. [L0359.056]
- A free-gift app with a plan of about 20 USD ended up at almost 100 USD per month once usage fees were added. Check usage-based fees before choosing an app. [L0694.028]
- Remove costly bundle or gift apps once revenue is good enough to switch. [L0694.028]
- Some apps offer a free trial or free plan badge in the listing, but billing starts after the trial. Check whether the trial or free tier ends in a paid plan. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1298.039]
- Bundle discounts from the Kaching app page grow with the number of apps installed: 10% off with 2 apps, 20% off with 3 apps, and 30% off with 4 or more apps, applied to all installed apps. Check the exact terms and whether the discount applies to your plan before counting on it in a budget. [L0861.042]
- Shopify's app bundle discount as shown in the admin in 2026: 2 apps installed gives 10% off, 3 apps gives 20% off, and 4 or more apps gives 30% off. [L0862.017]
- Use Bold Product Options and Bold Product Upsell on stores where the functionality is needed. Do not use Bold Discounts. Use promo codes promoted on the store instead of storewide discounts. *(older advice, 2021)* [L1279.043]
- Do not pay for a tool before sales arrive. Several starter apps have free trials, so avoid paying before sales arrive. [L1380.036]
- A currency converter app was switched off in a test without a visible change in conversion rate, because most ads targeted the US and the store sold in USD. Such apps can conflict with other apps, which may be a reason to remove them. [L0694.027]

### Vetting an app before installing

#### Search and compare

1. Open the Shopify App Store from the Shopify admin. [L1301.008]
2. Type the feature you need into the search bar, for example wishlist. [L1301.008]
3. Compare the results. Check the developer, and note that apps developed by Shopify are free. [L1301.008]
4. Open the app listing and review its demo video, screenshots, description, rating, number of reviews, and available pricing plans. [L1301.008]
5. Pick a plan and click Install to add the app to the store. [L1301.008]

- Search by the feature you need, such as wishlist, rather than by brand name. [L1301.007]
- The store front page has a search bar, a Popular with stores like yours section at the top, and trending and spotlighted app lists. [L1301.007]
- Search results can be narrowed with the checkbox See only compatible apps and with a pricing filter set to Free or Paid. The default sort order is Most relevant. [L1300.005]
- The Shopify App Store is organised into these categories: Store design, Orders and shipping, Sales and conversion, Marketing, Inventory management, Productivity, Reporting, Finances, Customer support, Finding products, Places to sell and Trust and security. Customer support, for example, contains support ticket apps. [L1300.004]

#### Ratings and reviews

- Before choosing a third-party app, check its ratings, the number of reviews and whether the reviews are recent. Constant Contact's Shopify connector was reported at $5 per month with 4.3 stars from 58 reviews. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0469.060]
- To pick a Shopify app, search the Shopify App Store for the task and sort by the number of reviews and star ratings. The app with the most five-star reviews is a good first option. [L0667.046]
- A wishlist app with a rating of 4.9 out of 5 stars from more than 1,900 reviews is an example of a well-reviewed app. Ratings and review counts together help compare competing apps for the same feature. [L1301.009]
- Shopify app search results show each listing's star rating, review count and free trial length, which helps compare apps in the same category. In 2021, the Easy Tabs - Product Tabs listing by NexusMedia showed 4.9 stars from 742 reviews, a 7-day free trial and a price of $5 per month. Compare several listings before installing one. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.036]
- Poor apps usually show bad reviews in the Shopify App Store. *(older advice, 2022)* [L0429.021]

#### Vetting procedure for a wholesale or sourcing app

1. Compare ratings of candidate apps in the Shopify App Store. [L0671.010]
2. Read the actual written reviews to see whether they look genuine. [L0671.010]
3. Look for video reviews on YouTube before installing. [L0671.010]

*(older advice, 2021)* [L0671.010]

#### Developer and integration

- Choose tools with the deepest Shopify integration, such as Klaviyo. [L0469.061]
- Augmented reality room-preview apps are not necessary for most niches. They need extra apps, coding skill and budget, and the preview may not match the product photos. [L0469.064]

### Installing an app

#### Procedure as taught by Source B

1. In the Shopify admin, open the Apps menu. If no apps are installed, click Visit Shopify app store, which opens in a new tab. [L1300.006]
2. If Shopify asks you to choose an account, select the store account again. [L1300.006]
3. Type the app name in the search bar and open the correct listing. In the example, Easy Tab, Easy Tabs - Product Tabs by NexusMedia. [L1300.006]
4. Click Add app. [L1300.006]
5. On the confirmation screen, read the list of access rights and click Install app. [L1300.006]
6. Open the app. It loads its own admin interface, where you start the free trial if needed and complete the setup. [L1300.006]

#### Procedure as taught by Source C

1. Open the Shopify search bar, go to Apps, then All recommended apps, and scroll down to the Shopify App Store. [L1297.001]
2. Search for the app by name and open the listing. [L1297.001]
3. Click Add app, then Install app. [L1297.001]
4. Log in to an existing account or sign up, which connects the app to your store. [L1297.001]

- To install a Shopify app, open Apps in the Shopify admin, click all recommended apps, scroll down and open the Shopify app store. Search the app name, click Add app and then Install app. [L0477.034]

#### Permissions and data

- On the Shopify app install confirmation screen, clicking Install app also accepts Shopify's Terms of Service. Read the list of access rights on that screen before you confirm. [L1300.035]
- The install confirmation lists what the app may access: view Shopify account data, view products, manage your Online Store and view details. Removing the app starts a request to erase your customers' personal data after 48 hours. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.008]
- Some apps place features into the main Shopify areas, while most run through their own admin interface. After installation, open the app and manage its settings there. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.009]
- To remove an app, go to Shopify admin > Apps, open the app, and click Uninstall. [L1301.012]

### Working with app developers

- Contact the developer named on the app's store listing for any change to how the app displays on your site, instead of editing code yourself if you are not a coder. For Easy Tabs the developer's support address is support@nexusmedia-ua.com. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.011]
- Procedure for a placement change:
  1. Find the developer's contact details on the app's store listing. [L1300.012]
  2. Reply to the developer's welcome email, which keeps the earlier conversation in one thread. [L1300.012]
  3. Ask the developer to install the app in a way that fits your theme and places it where it should appear on the product pages. [L1300.012]
  4. Wait for the developer to complete the change, which in the example took up to 12 hours. [L1300.012]
- In the example, the app developer carried out the requested placement change within 12 hours and at no charge. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1300.013]

### Page speed and app weight

#### How apps slow a store

- Apps that place content on the storefront inject extra code and JavaScript that must load with every page. A large number of such apps slows page load, which can lower conversion rates and hurt organic search rankings. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1298.002]
- Each installed app adds JavaScript that runs on every page load, which slows the page. Keep the number of apps low and remove unused apps along with their code. [L1345.035, L1410.011]
- Some apps use lazy loading to defer their scripts so they do not slow the first view. Every installed app still adds JavaScript, so check the impact on load time before installing. [L1410.048]
- Many stores run around 30 apps while only 2 or 3 are fundamental. Remove apps that are not essential to the business. Each app adds code and load time, especially on the cart and checkout pages. [L0390.036]
- Each app added can slow the store, so use as few as possible. [L1283.082]
- Apps can slow page load and add monthly cost. [L1116.020]
- The number of Shopify apps does not determine slowdown. One poorly coded app can damage performance and user experience, while many lightweight, well-coded apps may have almost no effect. *(older advice, 2022)* [L0429.021]
- Use lightweight apps that add little code and do not change how the site operates. Apps that alter the site structure can drastically increase load times. [L1098.012]
- Within one app category, page-speed impact differs. When features are similar, choose the app that is lighter on the site. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1298.003]
- Apps with similar functions differ hugely in speed, so test alternatives before choosing. Talk To is a light chat app with minimal page speed impact, while TDO Chat was described as the slowest chat app on Shopify. Pop-up apps that load asynchronously (separately from the page) have less impact than those that block loading. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1266.019]
- Chat apps on Shopify differ greatly in page speed impact even when they offer the same function. For a budget-limited early-stage store, a light and cheaper option such as Talk To is a reasonable choice. Replacing a slow chat app is usually the single biggest quick speed fix. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1266.038]

#### Speed check after installing

1. Install the app. [L1098.013]
2. Run a speed check on the store. [L1098.013]
3. Test the load on a 3G connection. [L1098.013]
4. Confirm the site is still easy to use. [L1098.013]
5. If it slows the store, remove the app. [L1098.013]

[L1098.013]

### App audit

- Apps are almost always the biggest cause of slow page speed on Shopify. Remove apps that are not used. Remove the leftover code of uninstalled apps from the theme, because uninstalling an app does not remove its script from the site. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1266.017]
- Removing an app from Shopify does not always remove its injected code. Contact the app developer to ask where code must be deleted. [L1283.082]

#### Audit procedure

1. List every app that adds front-end code: chat widgets, upsell widgets, option apps, filters, header bars, pop-ups, JavaScript-based tools. Back-end apps such as accounting connectors do not affect page speed. [L1266.018]
2. For each app, ask whether it is still used regularly. If not, delete it. [L1266.018]
3. If it is needed, check whether a faster alternative with similar function and cost exists, and replace it. [L1266.018]
4. Check the theme code after deletion to confirm no script of the removed app remains. [L1266.018]
5. Consider replacing simple app functions, such as banners or simple elements, with code written into the theme, which is more efficient. Keep apps only for complex functions such as live chat. [L1266.018]

*(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1266.018]

- Hire a Shopify developer with page speed experience to audit the store once and fix the technical issues: apps, code, lazy loading and theme. This is treated as a worthwhile one-time investment rather than something to learn yourself. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1266.021]

### Uninstalling an app

- When uninstalling an app that slowed the store, message the app developer through the Shopify App Store first. They can confirm whether extra code was added that must be removed to restore load speed. [L1098.014]
- Removing an app does not always remove its injected code, so ask the developer where code must be deleted. [L1283.082]
- Remove apps that are not essential to the business. [L0390.036]
- Remove an app that conflicts with other apps. [L0694.027]
- Remove costly bundle or gift apps once revenue is good enough to switch. [L0694.028]
- Cancel apps that do not lead to sales. [L0359.056]

### Finding apps that competitors use

#### Browser inspection procedure

1. Open the competitor's store in Chrome. [L1345.089]
2. Right-click and choose Inspect. [L1345.089]
3. Use the select-element tool and click the bundle block on the page. [L1345.089]
4. Read the element's code and search for the app name, such as 'bundle' or the app's name in the script. [L1345.089]
5. Note the app name and check its listing on the Shopify App Store. [L1345.089]

#### Tools that show competitor apps

- Ali Hunter (Chrome extension), PPSpy, and Koala Inspector show which apps competitor stores use. Koala Inspector may require an upgrade for full app data. [L1345.090]

### Theme features, custom code or a freelancer

#### Use the theme first

- Use the built-in theme features before paying for a separate app or a custom developer job for the same function. Review collection still needs a separate app, because the theme does not provide it. [L0973.015]
- Some features built into a theme can replace functions that would otherwise need separate paid Shopify apps. Checking a theme's native features before buying apps can reduce monthly app costs. [L0974.008]
- Simple site elements such as banners or small on-page features can often be coded directly into the theme by an inexpensive developer. This is usually more efficient than a script-heavy app. Complex functions such as live chat still normally require an app. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1266.039]

#### Custom code for simple features

- For a simple custom feature, ask an LLM for custom Shopify section code instead of installing an app or hiring a developer. [L1116.020]
- Use apps only for features that would be too hard to code yourself. Build simple features with on-site code. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0853.089]

#### Hiring a freelancer

- Get a custom feature built when paid apps are too expensive:
  1. Post a job for a Shopify developer on Upwork. [L0968.051]
  2. Describe the function you need and link to the apps you want to emulate. [L0968.051]
  3. Hire a developer to build it into your store for a one-time cost. [L0968.051]

*(older advice, 2021)* [L0968.051]

- Platforms for hiring Shopify developers for custom code, such as custom buttons and external service integration, include Fiverr.com and StoreTasker.com. [L0276.063]
- Stack Overflow is a technical Q&A site useful for finding fixes to specific coding problems. Reddit can also be used to ask for recommendations from developers, though answers can be harsh for beginners. [L0709.037]

## Domain, Business Email and Phone Setup

### Domain: Choosing and Buying the Domain

- Use a ChatGPT thinking model with deep research enabled to generate brand names. Deep research browses the live internet and checks which domains are actually free. Without it, most suggested.com domains turn out to be taken. [L0989.006]
- Namecheap is the domain registrar used in this approach. It handles the purchase, domain privacy and DNS records. Any other domain provider can be used instead. [L0989.020]
- Older advice (2019): buy the domain through Shopify if you want everything linked from the start. It costs a few dollars more than an outside registrar such as Namecheap, which is cheaper. [L0357.054, older 2019]
- If the domain still does not link after the DNS records are updated, take a screenshot of the DNS records together with the exact error message. Paste both into a ChatGPT thinking model to get troubleshooting help. [L0989.027]

### Google Workspace: Prices and Plans

Prices in the sources differ by year, currency and purchase route. Use the live pricing page before you sign up, because prices change. [L1197.002, L1049.020]

| Source | Price per user per month | Note | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source A (current) | $7 on the annual plan | Recommended starting plan, Business Starter | L0119.004, L1196.003, L1197.004 |
| Source A (current) | $8.40 on the flexible plan | Same plan, no long-term commitment. Difference to annual: $1.40 per month | L1196.004, L1197.004 |
| Source A (2024) | $6 | Price shown through the course link | L0360.075, L0806.043 |
| Source D (2026, Singapore list) | Starter S$9.40 on annual billing | Save 16% with a one-year commitment. Standard S$18.80, Plus S$29.40, Enterprise on contact | L1327.003 |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | About $12 | Varies with currency and region | L0566.005 |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | About $6 to $7 for a single-user mailbox | Quoted at the time of recording. Check current pricing before budgeting | L0574.001 |

- Business Starter on the annual plan costs $7 per user per month and is billed monthly. It requires a 12-month commitment. Choose it only if the account will be kept for at least one year. [L1197.005, L1196.003]
- Business Starter on the flexible plan has no long-term commitment and can be cancelled or downgraded at any time. [L1197.004]
- Buy through the pricing page link, not the general signup page. The general signup page shows no pricing and defaults to a higher-priced tier. [L1196.003, L1197.003]
- Start the trial only from the pricing page link. Choose Business Starter at $7 per month and click Start a trial. [L1197.003]
- Business Starter includes 30 GB pooled storage per user, custom business email, and Gemini in Gmail, with a 14-day free trial at no cost (Singapore list). [L1327.003]
- Check the plan name carefully. Higher-priced plans appear as the default. [L0806.043]

### Google Workspace: Free Trial, Billing and Seats

- Google Workspace starts with a free trial of at least 14 days. [L0119.005, L1145.005]
- Billing can be added during the trial or after it. No charge is made before the trial ends, and Google sends a notice before the trial expires, so billing can be left until then. [L0566.015]
- Billing begins 14 days after signup on both the annual and flexible plans. The annual plan is still billed monthly, so the monthly charge stays at $7 and is not paid up front. Users can be added or removed as needed. [L1196.005, L1197.006]
- If billing is never activated, the mailbox stops receiving mail when the trial ends, and existing messages cannot be retrieved afterward. [L1145.005]
- Billing setup asks for the billing address and a credit card on file. [L1197.008, L1196.008]
- Create a payment profile with the address and add a credit card on file. Click Agree and continue. [L1196.008]
- Each additional paid user license costs $7 per month. Aliases are not paid users and do not add to the monthly price. [L1196.006]
- A Google Workspace account includes up to 30 alternate email addresses (aliases) at no extra cost. The user still signs in only with the primary address, and aliases are used to send and receive mail. [L1196.006, L1197.014]
- Do not click "buy more licenses" in the admin panel when you only need extra email addresses. Every added license costs $7 per month. Aliases on the existing account cover multiple addresses without this cost. Add paid users only when an employee needs their own separate inbox. [L1196.007]
- When only the first account is needed during setup, click Cancel on the prompt to buy more licenses. [L1197.007]
- Creating extra user accounts is not needed during initial setup and can be skipped. Additional team members can be added later from the admin console. [L0566.016]

### Google Workspace: Before You Sign Up

- Sign up for the $7 per month plan first, before creating any other Google Workspace account for the same domain. [L0667.044]
- If a Google Workspace account was created for the domain before signing up through the $7 per month link, cancel that account and sign up again with the $7 plan. Google will otherwise report that the domain is already in use. [L0667.045]
- Create the business email address before signing up for Quo, because Quo asks for a business email and uses it to create the account. The same address is then used to log in to the app. [L0338.012]
- Google changes the signup steps often. When creating a new business email account, follow the current signup screens rather than older guides. Existing Google Workspace business email accounts keep working and do not need to be changed after such an update. [L1049.020]
- Signing up with a domain you already own leaves that domain and any existing website unchanged. Only the mail routing of the domain is connected to Google. [L0566.020]

### Google Workspace: Signup Procedures

**Signup via the pricing link (Source A):**

1. Open the Google Workspace pricing page through the course link, not the general signup page. [L1196.008]
2. Under Business Starter ($7 per month), click Start a trial. [L1196.008]
3. Enter the business name, choose 1 for number of employees, and select the region where the business operates, then click Next. [L1196.008]
4. Enter the contact first and last name and a personal email address as a backup login, then click Next. [L1196.008]
5. Answer yes to having a domain name, enter the domain without https:// or www (for example yourbrand.com), confirm it, and click Next. [L1196.008]
6. Set the sign-in address to sales@yourdomain.com, choose a password, and click Agree and continue. [L1196.008]
7. Select the annual plan (or flexible if you want no commitment), then click Try free for 14 days. [L1196.008]
8. Create a payment profile by entering the address and adding a credit card on file, then click Agree and continue. [L1196.008]
9. Click Get started. [L1196.008]
10. At the domain host question, keep Shopify if the domain was bought through Shopify, then click Continue. [L1196.008]

**Signup for a business email domain (Source A, shorter version):**

1. Open the pricing page through the link, choose Business Starter ($7 per user per month), and click Start a trial. [L1197.008]
2. Enter the business name, the number of employees (select just you for a one-person account), and the region where the business operates, then click Next. [L1197.008]
3. Enter the name of the person setting up the account and a personal email address as a backup for account recovery, then click Next. [L1197.008]
4. When asked whether the business has a domain name, answer yes, enter the domain without https:// or www (for example domain.com), confirm it, and click Next. [L1197.008]
5. Set the primary sign-in address to sales@yourdomain.com, choose a password, and click Agree and continue. [L1197.008]
6. Select the Annual plan ($7 per month, 12-month commitment) or the Flexible plan ($8.40 per month), then click Try free for 14 days. [L1197.008]
7. Create a payment profile with the billing address and add a credit card on file, then click Agree and continue. [L1197.008]
8. Click Get started to begin the domain verification steps. [L1197.008]

**Signup with the domain question and admin steps (Source B, older 2021-22):**

1. Open workspace.google.com (or search for Google Workspace) and click Start free trial. [L0566.007]
2. Enter the business name as you want it to appear; spaces are allowed. [L0566.007]
3. Select 'Just you' as the number of employees if you work alone. [L0566.007]
4. Select the country where the business will operate, not necessarily where you live. The country cannot be changed after signup. A warning that your IP address points to another country can be ignored if the selected country is correct. [L0566.007]
5. Enter your own first and last name. You become the account admin. [L0566.007]
6. Enter a current personal email address. Google sends the welcome message there and uses it for account recovery. [L0566.007]
7. When asked whether the business has a domain, choose 'I have a domain' and click Next. [L0566.007]
8. Type the domain name without https:// or slashes (for example example.com), confirm it is the domain to use, and click Next. [L0566.007]
9. Answer the optional Google marketing questions as you prefer and continue. [L0566.007]
10. Choose the username prefix (support, sales, hello or info). The full address becomes prefix@yourdomain. [L0566.007]
11. Create a password of at least 8 characters and store it somewhere secure. Complete the robot check and click Agree and continue. [L0566.007]
12. Click the blue button to continue into setup, then sign in again with the new address and password. [L0566.007]
13. If Google asks you to verify the account, enter your country and phone number, choose Text message (SMS) or Automated voice call, click Get code, enter the code and click Verify. [L0566.007]
14. Accept the terms of service and privacy policy. [L0566.007]
15. In the Google Admin console (admin.google.com), click ACTIVATE under Activate Gmail. This step can take up to 60 minutes. [L0566.007]
16. Click 'Sign in to activate'. Google detects GoDaddy as your domain host. Sign in to GoDaddy if asked, then click Connect. [L0566.007]
17. Wait while Google verifies the domain and updates the mail settings. The screen shows 'Less than a minute remaining' near the end. [L0566.007]
18. If you see 'There was a problem updating settings in GoDaddy.com', click 'Sign in to activate' again. If it fails repeatedly, click 'Switch verification method' and try another method. [L0566.007]
19. When Gmail is activated, skip 'Create new users' for now. [L0566.007]
20. Either click 'Set up billing' now or later. Google emails you before the 14-day trial ends. Billing requires your name, address and payment method details. [L0566.007]
21. Click 'Send test email' to confirm that mail works. [L0566.007]
22. Log in to gmail.com with the new business address to access the inbox. [L0566.007]

**Signup for a Singapore-based store (Source D, 2026):**

1. Search for Google Workspace and open the sign-up page with the free trial. [L1327.004]
2. Start the free trial and choose the Business Starter plan. [L1327.004]
3. Enter the business name (the store or brand name) and the domain name. [L1327.004]
4. Enter the number of employees (Just you for a solo operator) and the region (Singapore in the example). [L1327.004]
5. Enter the first and last name of the admin. The person who creates the account becomes the Google Workspace account admin. [L1327.004]
6. Finish creating the account, then use the business email in the Shopify store contact details. [L1327.004]

**Signup field notes:**

- The Google Workspace sign-up asks for the number of employees, with the options Just you, 2-9, 10-99, 100-299 and 300+. A solo store owner picks Just you, and the answer does not block the setup of a single business email address. [L0566.019]
- Entering https:// or www in the domain field creates a wrong domain setup. Enter only the bare domain, such as domain.com. [L1197.012]
- The domain step appears as 'Does your business have a domain?'. Select 'I have a domain' when you already own a domain. Select the buy-a-domain or free page-domain options only if you have no domain yet. [L0566.009]

### Google Workspace: Connecting the Domain's Mail (Activation)

- Activation routes the domain's email to Gmail. In the Google Admin console, go to Getting started, then click ACTIVATE under 'Activate Gmail for [yourdomain]'. The step is estimated at 5 minutes, with a possible wait of up to 60 minutes. [L0566.008]
- If your domain is registered with GoDaddy, Google detects it automatically during Gmail activation and offers an automated connection. For a larger domain provider the automated option usually works too. Smaller providers may not be detected and need an alternative verification method. [L0566.010]
- Connection with GoDaddy is confirmed automatically. Google updates the domain's mail settings and then verifies they are in place. The check can take a few minutes, so wait on the screen rather than closing it. [L0566.011]
- The error 'There was a problem updating settings in GoDaddy.com' is common and usually means the two systems did not sync. It is not a sign of a setup mistake. Retry 'Sign in to activate' before changing the verification method. [L0566.012]
- Google may ask for phone verification when it detects unusual activity on a new account. The code can be sent by text message (SMS) or automated voice call. The phone number is used only for security. [L0566.013]
- Once the MX record for your domain points to Google (smtp.google.com with priority 1), all incoming mail for that domain is delivered to Gmail. Any older mailbox or forwarding setup for the same domain stops receiving new mail. Check what is still in use before switching the record. [L1196.020]
- Send a test email in the Google Admin console after verification. This confirms that the domain's mail routes correctly before you use the address with suppliers and customers. [L0566.017]

**Post-activation checklist (Google Admin console, Getting started page, in this order):**

1. Set up billing (5 min) [L0566.024]
2. Send test email (after Gmail is activated and the domain is verified) [L0566.024]
3. Create new users (5 min) [L0566.024]
4. Try out Gmail and other apps (1 min) [L0566.024]
5. Set up your mobile devices (10 min) [L0566.024]
6. Migrate your email and documents, importing existing email, contacts and data (1 hr) [L0566.024]
7. Enhance your mail security, adding protection against spam and phishing (5 min) [L0566.024]

### Email Addresses and Aliases

- Use professional addresses on your own store domain instead of a personal inbox when contacting suppliers, and keep using them for customers. A simple setup uses three addresses on one account: sales@, returns@, and firstname@ on the store domain. [L1197.001]
- Email aliases deliver all messages to a single inbox. You select which address to send from for each message, with no need to monitor separate accounts. [L0119.009]
- Alternate email addresses (aliases) cannot be used to sign in to Google Workspace. Always sign in with the primary address (for example sales@yourdomain.com) and its password. Aliases receive and send mail through that primary account. [L1196.022]
- Use the same Google Workspace email account for Google Search Console, Google Analytics, Google Merchant Center, and Google Ads so that all Google tools connect and share data cleanly. [L0171.002]

**Add alias addresses (Source A, first version):**

1. In the Google Workspace admin panel, click the blue underlined name of the sales@ user. [L1196.012]
2. In the left navigation, click Add alternate emails. [L1196.012]
3. Enter returns as the first alias, which becomes returns@yourdomain.com. [L1196.012]
4. Enter a personal alias using your first name (adding your last name is also recommended), for example firstname@yourdomain.com. [L1196.012]
5. Click Save and check that all addresses are listed as alternate emails under the main account. [L1196.012]

**Add alias addresses (Source A, second version):**

1. After the welcome screen for the new account, click I understand, then click Cancel on the prompt asking to buy more licenses. [L1197.013]
2. Open the Google Workspace admin panel and click the name of the primary account. [L1197.013]
3. In the left navigation, click Add alternate emails. [L1197.013]
4. Add the alternate emails returns and firstname (for example, the first name of the operator), each created on the store domain. Include the last name in the alias name if it is used for supplier contact. [L1197.013]
5. Check that the list shows sales@, returns@, and firstname@ on the store domain, then click Save. [L1197.013]

**Add alias addresses (Source A, older 2020 version, same steps):**

1. Open Google Workspace Admin. [L0119.008]
2. Click your primary user (sales@yourdomain.com). [L0119.008]
3. Select Add alternate emails. [L0119.008]
4. Add support@yourdomain.com or returns@yourdomain.com. [L0119.008]
5. Add yourfirstname@yourdomain.com. [L0119.008]
6. Save changes. [L0119.008]
7. Open Gmail and log in as sales@yourdomain.com. [L0119.008]
8. Click the gear icon and select See all settings. [L0119.008]
9. Go to Accounts tab. [L0119.008]
10. Edit display name to your store name. [L0119.008]
11. Add additional "Send mail as" addresses for support/returns email. [L0119.008]
12. Add your personal email as another "Send mail as" option. [L0119.008]
13. Set replies to send from the same address the original email was sent to. [L0119.008]

**Set up sending from the aliases in Gmail (Source A, first version):**

1. Click the gear icon in Gmail, then click See all settings. [L1196.014]
2. Open the Accounts tab. Under Send mail as, click Edit next to the sales address. [L1196.014]
3. Change the name from the generic account name to the brand name (for example the store name), then click Save changes. [L1196.014]
4. Click Add another email address, enter the name and the alias returns@yourdomain.com, then click Next step. [L1196.014]
5. Under When replying to a message, change the default from Always reply from the default address to Reply from the same address the message was sent to. [L1196.014]
6. Repeat steps 4 and 5 for the personal alias firstname@yourdomain.com. [L1196.014]
7. Click Compose, then choose the From address (sales, returns or personal) to send from it. [L1196.014]

**Set up send-as aliases in Gmail (Source A, second version):**

1. In Gmail, click the gear icon, then click See all settings. [L1197.015]
2. Open the Accounts tab. [L1197.015]
3. Under Send mail as, click edit on the primary address, change the name to the store name (for example, the store's name as customers should see it), and click Save changes. [L1197.015]
4. Click Add another email address, enter the name (for example, Returns - Store Name) and the returns@ address, then click Next step. [L1197.015]
5. In the settings for returns@, set When replying to a message to Reply from the same address the message was sent to, then click Next step. [L1197.015]
6. Add the firstname@ address with the first and last name, then click Next step. [L1197.015]
7. Open Compose and select the From address (sales@, returns@, or firstname@) before sending. [L1197.015]
8. Leave Check mail from other accounts empty, because the aliases were already added in Google Workspace. [L1197.015]

**Reply and sending mistakes:**

- Gmail's default is to reply from the primary sales address even when a message arrived at the returns or personal alias. Change the reply setting to reply from the same address the message was sent to, so customers see a consistent sender. [L1196.015, L1197.016]
- Do not add anything under Check mail from other accounts, because the aliases already receive mail through Google Workspace. [L1196.015, L1197.015]

**Use the account on a phone:**

- To use all addresses on a phone, add the account to the phone's email app using the primary sales@yourdomain.com address and its password. Once added, the app can send and receive from all aliases on the account. No separate setup is needed for each alias on the phone. [L1196.016]
- In any email app on the phone, add an account using the primary address (sales@yourdomain.com) and its password. Send and receive mail from all three addresses, because the aliases are linked to the same account. [L1197.017]
- After activation, the business address can be used at gmail.com or in desktop and mobile mail apps such as Apple Mail or Outlook, by adding the Gmail account to the app. This lets you send and receive on a phone or computer. [L0566.014]

### Google Workspace: Branding, Apps and Overview

- Google Workspace provides professional email using your custom domain with the standard Gmail interface and admin dashboard. [L0119.003]
- Google Workspace hosts business email on your own domain and is managed through the Gmail interface. The Business Starter plan is the recommended starting plan. [L1197.002]
- Google Workspace provides a custom-domain business email, Gmail, Calendar, Meet, Drive and Docs under one paid account. It is used to run the business email address for the store. [L0566.006]
- Google Workspace provides business email on your own domain plus tools such as Drive and Calendar. It is the usual way to set up a professional business email address for a store. [L1095.005]
- Send a test email in the Google Admin console after verification to confirm that the domain's mail routes correctly before using the address with suppliers and customers. [L0566.017]
- Upload a custom logo in the Google Workspace admin settings so the account shows the store's logo in Gmail instead of the standard Google logo. Download the logo from your store, upload it under Custom logo under the profile settings of the main account, and click Save. [L1196.021, L1197.019]
- Older advice (2020s, course link): the Business Starter plan is recommended for a new store. Buy it through the pricing page link, because the general signup page does not show prices. [L1196.003]

### Email Tools: Filters and Scheduling

- Gmail filters and rules can apply labels and sort incoming emails automatically, so the assistant can action messages without sorting them by hand. Combine these rules with colour-coded priority labels so urgent messages surface first. [L1000.077]
- Boomerang for Gmail schedules emails to send later and can send a reminder back to you if a recipient has not replied within a set number of days. The reminder is used to track supplier actions that would otherwise be forgotten. [L0910.040]
- Boomerang's scheduled sending is optional for business email. In the store described, suppliers and customers did not object to emails sent late at night or early in the morning. The reminder feature is the more useful part for tracking supplier follow-ups. [L0910.059]
- Boomerang for Gmail, a browser plugin linked to Gmail, lets you write emails at night and schedule their delivery for a set time in the recipient's time zone, such as 9 a.m. US time. Use it to show suppliers your email arrives during their business hours, for example by scheduling for 9:03 a.m. [L1441.021]

### Security and Gemini in Google Workspace

- Enable two-step verification for all Google Workspace users. It sends a code by text message when logging in on a new device. This protects the account from anyone who obtains the password. [L1196.018, L1197.018]
- The other optional items during setup, such as the Gemini sidebar and workspace app downloads, can be skipped or enabled later. [L1196.018]
- The Gemini AI sidebar in Gmail can summarize emails, draft emails and auto replies, and find information in the inbox. It is included with the Google Workspace account and can be minimized or closed. It is optional and not required for the email setup. [L1196.019, L1197.023]
- Gemini in Gmail is included in the Starter plan (Singapore list). [L1327.003]

### Alternative Mail Provider

- Zoho Workplace is a common alternative to Google Workspace for business email with custom domain aliases. [L1285.043, older 2024 source]

### Phone Systems: Choosing a Number and System

| Option | Price | Number type | Trial and included features | Region | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Quo (formerly OpenPhone), Starter | $15 per user per month, annual billing | One number, local or toll-free. Toll-free is included at no extra cost | Calling, SMS, automatic voicemail transcripts, "Try for free" at signup | USA and Canada | L0122.004, L0122.005, L0338.004, L0338.005, L0338.006, L0338.014 |
| OpenPhone Starter (same product, earlier name) | $15 per user per month, annual ($180 upfront for 12 months, renews at $180 each year) | One local or toll-free number | 7-day free trial via Try it free | USA | L0700.007, L0700.014 |
| Google Voice | $10 per month (added to a Google Workspace account) | Local only, chosen by area code. No toll-free | Managed in the Google Voice app on phone and computer | US | L0700.005, L0700.006, L0700.013 |
| Grasshopper | Not stated in the sources | Typically 1-800 format | Calls and SMS, text-back button | Not stated | L0286.027, L1472.054 |
| Mighty Call | Not stated in the sources (2020: 50% off first three months through a promotional link, older) | Business phone numbers | Voicemail transcripts, call routing | Not stated | L0852.119, L0970.086 |

- Quo was renamed from OpenPhone. The acquired service, features and price are unchanged, and existing OpenPhone accounts were moved over to Quo. [L0338.003]
- For a single store with one business number, the Quo Starter plan is enough. A higher plan is unnecessary for this setup. [L0338.013]
- Toll-free numbers start with 888 or 855. Google Voice does not offer them, while OpenPhone includes one local or toll-free number in its plan. [L0700.006]
- Choosing OpenPhone for a toll-free number costs about $5 more per month than Google Voice. Choose Google Voice if a local number is acceptable. Start with the Starter plan of either tool and upgrade only when more features are needed while scaling. [L0700.008]
- Choose a toll-free number, such as an 855 number, on the Quo Starter plan because it looks more professional on a store website than a local number. The difference is minor, but the toll-free number is included in the Starter plan at no extra cost. [L0338.005]
- Business phone apps such as Google Voice and OpenPhone run on a personal phone and computer. Business calls ring through the app so they are recognizable. Outbound calls placed in the app show the business number, so no separate carrier plan is needed. [L0700.004]
- A business phone number with a companion app can ring on your phone, handle calls, and provide voice-to-text transcription of voicemail. It can also route calls to team members at different times of the day as the business grows. [L0941.045]
- A business phone number from a VoIP provider can ring through that provider's app on a cell phone, so business calls are easy to tell apart from personal ones. Landline routing depends on the provider, so confirm the setup directly with them. [L0853.008, older 2021]
- A phone-forwarding app can route the store's phone number to your cell phone, so customer calls are answered or returned quickly. It is useful when you choose to sell higher-priced products with a phone number on the site from day one. [L0835.052]

### Quo: Setup and Use

- Quo (formerly OpenPhone) is a cloud-based business phone system with iOS and Android mobile apps. It routes dedicated business numbers and messages through your existing smartphone. Incoming business calls are clearly labeled. [L0122.003]
- Quo is a business phone system with a mobile app that gives a store its own phone number. Calls placed through the app show the business number to the other side. Incoming calls to that number ring in the app, so the owner can still use a personal phone. [L0338.002]
- Quo's Starter plan includes one business phone number with a choice of local or toll-free, calling and messaging in the US and Canada, and automatic voicemail transcripts. [L0122.005, L0338.004]
- Quo's calling and messaging for business numbers works in the USA and Canada only. [L0338.006]
- Quo's Starter plan includes business messaging (SMS text) on the business number in addition to calling. Customers and suppliers can write to the same number as they call. [L0338.014]

**Sign up for Quo (first version):**

1. Click the Quo signup link. [L0122.008]
2. Select Try for free. [L0122.008]
3. Enter your business email address. [L0122.008]
4. Choose the Starter plan. [L0122.008]
5. Select your phone number type (local or toll-free). [L0122.008]

**Sign up for Quo and install the app (second version):**

1. Open the Quo sign-up page using the link provided with the lesson. [L0338.008]
2. Click Try for free. [L0338.008]
3. Sign up with a business email address. [L0338.008]
4. Answer the setup questions, including the desired phone number. The account is created instantly. [L0338.008]
5. Select the Starter plan and claim the included toll-free number. [L0338.008]
6. Install the Quo app from the App Store on iPhone or from the Android app store, then log in with the same account. The app works right after login. [L0338.008]

**Set up the Quo mobile app:**

1. Download the Quo app from the App Store or Google Play. [L0122.009]
2. Log in with your Quo account. [L0122.009]
3. Calls and messages begin working immediately. [L0122.009]

### Google Voice: Setup and Use

- Google Voice is added to an existing Google Workspace account that was set up earlier with the business email. It costs $10 per month, is managed in the Google Voice app on phone and computer, and offers only local numbers chosen by area code. Toll-free numbers are not offered. [L0700.005]
- Google Voice works well in the US but not while traveling abroad. [L0910.011]
- Google Voice serves as the business phone line, answered by the VA during business hours, while other inbound messages go into the help desk. [L1473.002]
- On Google Voice, a call recording starts only after the agent presses a key, and a recording notice plays to the caller. Staff often skip this step, so do not rely on Google Voice for consistent call recording. [L0910.058]
- Check the consent rules for recording calls in the countries where the store and its callers are located. [L0910.058]

**Google Voice setup for a US business number:**

1. Open the Google Voice sign-up page and confirm you are logged in to the Google Workspace account created with the business email. [L0700.013]
2. Scroll to the pricing section and choose the Starter plan at $10 per month. [L0700.013]
3. When prompted, select the location and area code you want. The number issued is a local number. [L0700.013]
4. Install the Google Voice app on your phone and computer to take inbound calls and place outbound calls. [L0700.013]

### OpenPhone (Quo): Setup with Toll-Free Line

**OpenPhone setup for a US business number with a toll-free line:**

1. Open the OpenPhone page, review the preview of how it works, and check the pricing. [L0700.014]
2. Click Try it free and create the account. The trial runs seven days, after which the Starter plan costs $15 per user per month. [L0700.014, L0700.007]
3. Choose the toll-free number offered in the plan during signup. [L0700.014]
4. Install the OpenPhone app on your phone and computer to take inbound calls and place outbound calls. [L0700.014]

### Mighty Call

- Mighty Call is a business phone app for a second number. Install it on a computer or phone so business calls ring in the app and can be told apart from personal calls. [L0664.073, older 2020]
- Mighty Call provides business phone numbers and call handling for stores. At the time of the call it offered a 50% discount for the first three months through a promotional link. [L0852.119, older 2020]
- Mighty Call provides business phone numbers and call handling. It replaced Grasshopper about a year and a half before the call. One reported issue was that it did not work with T-Mobile. Grasshopper is the alternative. [L0852.078, older 2020]
- Mighty Call is a phone service used for the store's call line, chosen over Grasshopper after testing in 2019. It is used to route calls to the business. [L0960.044, older 2019]
- Mighty Call was a cloud phone service used in 2019 to provide business phone numbers for online stores. [L1002.080, older 2019]
- Mighty Call is a phone system that can route calls to voicemail. If callers hear two voicemail messages, the routing logic is misconfigured. Contact Mighty Call with the exact day and time the problem happens so they can fix the setting. [L1090.057, older 2021]
- Mighty Call is a business phone service that sends voicemail transcripts right away, so messages can be read on the go and returned quickly. [L0970.086]
- Mighty Call with voice-to-text can turn voicemails into emails with a clickable phone number and email link to call or reply. [L1113.035, older 2022]
- Mighty Call is a virtual phone service with staff in the United States and the Philippines who answer calls at the same time. It works from any country, so a store owner living abroad can run phone support from the same number. [L1111.184, older 2020]
- A virtual phone service with staff in several countries can be used to answer calls from anywhere in the world. It allows a store owner in another country to run the same phone support. [L1111.079, older 2020]

### Grasshopper and Aircall

- Grasshopper provides a business phone number (typically 1-800 format). It displays to customers while routing calls and texts to your mobile phone through the Grasshopper app. This maintains a professional identity without exposing personal numbers. [L0286.027]
- Grasshopper is a virtual phone provider that handles both calls and SMS texting for a business number. It has a text-back button for incoming calls that sends an automatic message saying you missed the call and will respond soon. [L1472.054]
- Grasshopper is a provider for toll-free business numbers, used when a store wants an 800 number. [L0356.029, older 2019]
- Aircall integrates with Reamaze. Each incoming call is logged automatically, and the customer's order information appears on screen. This lets the team handle a call from one platform. [L0337.032]

### Skype, Text Free and Messaging Apps

- Skype was the most reliable phone solution while traveling abroad. It was compared with Google Voice, toll-free number forwarding and Grasshopper. Skype is a workaround for travel, not the general recommendation. [L0910.011]
- For SMS or sign-up numbers, use a free second number from the Text Free app instead of your personal phone. The app issues a free number tied to your email. The number stays active as long as you make a call roughly once a month. [L0820.046]
- Text-Free was used to get a phone number for a new Ads Manager without using a personal number. [L1381.034]
- WhatsApp can be used for free calls and messaging with customers, including international calls. Its encryption is a selling point for privacy-conscious buyers. It is common for customers in Europe and can be used alongside an SMS provider. [L1472.056]
- Business phone numbers for non-US residents can be obtained through options such as MightyCall, Skype or Google Voice. Open Phone is recommended for Canada-based sellers shipping to the US. If none work, ask in the community forum. [L0804.053, older 2023]

### Voicemail and Call Handling

- Use a phone service that sends an SMS or email notification whenever a voicemail is left. The notification should show the caller's number and let you play the recorded message. [L0909.006, older 2021-22]
- Divert unanswered calls from your business number to a prerecorded voicemail greeting through your phone provider. Use a toll-free number such as a US 800 number, or an Australian 1-300 number if you operate in Australia. [L0909.007, older 2021-22]
- Set up a professional-sounding voicemail for the store phone, either by hiring a voice actor on Fiverr or by having a friend record a scripted greeting. Avoid using the founder's own voice, because a third-party voice makes the business look like a larger company. [L0910.012]
- Grasshopper's text-back button sends an automatic message when a call is missed, saying you will respond soon. [L1472.054]
- The phone system's voicemail and transcript feature lets you read messages on the go and return calls quickly. [L0970.086, L0338.004]

#### Further details

- 1. Go to Google Workspace pricing page and select Business Starter, then click Start a trial. 2. Enter your business name. 3. Select "Just you" for number of employees. 4. Choose your region. 5. Enter a personal email address as recovery contact. 6. Confirm you own the domain and enter your store domain without https:// or www. 7. Create sales@yourdomain.com as your primary login email. 8. Set a strong password. 9. Select annual billing to lock in the $7/month rate. 10. Add your payment method; billing begins after the free trial expires. [L0119.006]

## Team Workspace, Project Management and Hiring Tools

### Google Workspace as the shared base

- Google Drive is the central store for company files, including SOPs, FAQs, response templates, passwords and contact information, and the whole team can reach it from anywhere. [L0230.004, L0926.005]
- Keep all company files in one organised, shared Drive folder that the owner and any assistant can open, so the business can be run from anywhere. [L0230.004, L1000.047, L1441.018]
- Google Drive is used for most business tasks and file organisation. [L0835.123]
- Google Drive, Google Sheets, Google Hangouts and Gmail together form a cloud business system. [L1000.047]
- A cloud folder system such as Dropbox can be used alongside Google Drive. [L1473.007]
- Google Sheets is a free, cloud-based spreadsheet comparable to Microsoft Excel. It needs only a free Google account and is reached at drive.google.com. [L0737.001]
- Google Sheets is stored in Google Drive and used to store and edit business data and to track orders and other records that automations can update. [L0437.004]
- Google Docs, Google Drive and Basecamp can hold a project document and SOPs for each supplier, and can be shared with an assistant. [L0359.053]
- Google Voice provides free phone service, and a US phone number can be set up through it. [L0230.004, L1515.022]
- Cloud tools for a location-independent business are Google Docs for documents, Dropbox for files, Slack for team communication and Skype for calls, plus a US phone number through Google Voice. [L1515.022]

### Chrome profile as the business workspace

- A dedicated Google Chrome profile can act as a shared business workspace. It holds bookmarks to dashboards, spreadsheets, supplier price lists, contracts and account logins, organised by category. [L1473.008]
- Each business or project can have its own browser window, and a new hire can be given the login to see the same setup. [L1473.008]
- Setup procedure:
  1. Create a Google Chrome profile for the business as early as possible. [L1473.009]
  2. Bookmark every tool, dashboard and document used during daily work, such as the Shopify dashboard and Slack. [L1473.009]
  3. Start using a help desk tool like Remaze and a task board like Asana, with Trello as a possible alternative to Asana. [L1473.009]
  4. Once the processes run efficiently, document each one as a standard operating procedure. [L1473.009]
  5. Write FAQs from past customer emails and from the manufacturer's website, rewritten in your own words rather than copied. [L1473.009]
  6. Create on-brand response templates for the VA's email replies to reduce mistakes. [L1473.009]
  7. Keep one spreadsheet with all account passwords and one with supplier and service contacts, so the VA can answer questions without the owner being the contact point. [L1473.009]
  8. When these are in place, the system is ready for hiring. [L1473.009]

### Shared templates, copies and checklists

- Any shared Google document, whether Docs or Sheets, should be copied into your own Drive before you edit it. The shared original cannot be edited by you, and anyone with access to a shared version can see what you enter. [L0996.004]
- Procedure to work in a shared Google Sheets tracker (older: 2021-22):
  1. Open the shared Google Sheets link. [L0996.003]
  2. Click File in the top menu. [L0996.003]
  3. Choose Make a copy. [L0996.003]
  4. Save the copy in your own Google Drive and work only in that copy. [L0996.003]
- If copying a Google file feels too complex, use the Excel version of the tracker on your desktop instead. Choose whichever format fits your working style. [L0996.005]
- The research tracker exists as a Google Sheets version and a Microsoft Excel version. The Excel file can be downloaded and used on the desktop, on Windows or Mac. (older: 2021-22) [L0996.002]
- Keep your own copy of any shared checklist spreadsheet, either by copying it to your own drive or downloading it as Excel (preferred) or PDF. Edits then do not affect other users. (older: 2021-22) [L1145.003]
- To reuse a shared Google Doc template, choose File > Make a copy. In the Make a copy dialog you can keep the same sharing ("Share it with the same people"), copy comments and suggestions, and include resolved comments and suggestions before pressing Make a copy. Edit only your copy, not the shared original. [L1323.016]

### Team communication

- Slack is the internal communication platform for instant team notifications and task assignments. It also sends abandoned cart alerts and organises group messages. [L0230.006]
- Slack is used for daily team communication, with separate channels for work and a casual channel for team chat. Teams split across time zones use it as a shared office. [L0337.029]
- Slack is a team messaging app that can be used free of charge even without employees. It works as a personal notes space and receives instant notifications from automations, so the owner does not need to check email for every event. [L0437.006]
- Slack is a team messaging tool with channels, direct messages and group chats. It is used for internal team communication, including reporting outages and status updates. (older: 2021) [L0469.065]
- Slack is used for daily VA communication instead of email, because it allows near-instant replies and constant availability. It keeps VAs engaged and makes it easy to check progress during the day. [L0921.018]
- Slack is a team chat tool for communicating with remote team members and virtual assistants. [L0926.006]
- Slack is used only for internal communication, one-on-one and in group channels, and keeps a searchable message history. Outside communication with customers stays in the help desk. Slack is preferred over Asana messaging, which was slow and gave poor notifications, and over Skype or Facebook Messenger for team chat. [L1473.005]
- Keep the channels separate: customer messages go through the help desk, order and task management runs in Asana, and internal team talk runs in Slack. This makes it clear where each kind of work lives. [L1473.006]
- Use WhatsApp as the main direct channel for quick questions from the team. Slack and Discord are alternatives. [L1431.013]
- Source positions differ on the quick-question channel: one source uses WhatsApp for quick team questions [L1431.013], while another uses Slack for all internal talk with a searchable history [L1473.005]. WhatsApp fits fast one-line questions; Slack fits internal work that must stay searchable and separate from customer messages. [L1431.013, L1473.005]
- Google Hangouts can be used to chat with a candidate, and for team chat that appears inside Gmail. It was also used for face-to-face team meetings. (older: 2020-22 advice) [L1000.035, L1441.018]
- Skype calls that the assistant can listen to let the assistant learn the owner's tone when speaking with customers. [L1000.035]

### Program community and coaching logistics

#### Discord (Source A program)

- Discord is the community platform because other platforms were full of distractions and could not guarantee that members would see each other's posts. The space is dedicated to one community, where members ask questions, answer them and share wins. [L0517.002]
- Discord works like an instant messaging service. The server is split into sections and channels in the sidebar, each for a different topic or program stage. [L0517.003]
- Module-specific channels: questions about Module Zero go in the Module Zero chat thread, and questions about Module One go in the channel named 'One Niche Selection'. Search previous posts first, since the answer may already exist. [L0517.004]
- The 'Social' channel, at the bottom of the channel list, is a free chat where members connect with each other in real time on any topic. [L0517.005]
- Private or personal questions go by email to the source's own site Questions that would help the whole community go to the matching section of the Discord, where the coach or other members answer them. [L0517.009]
- Discord can be used in a web browser, in a mobile browser, or through the Discord mobile app, which keeps the community accessible on the phone at all times. [L0517.010]
- Procedure to join the members-only Discord community:
  1. Open the join link provided below the lesson, which opens Discord. [L0517.001]
  2. Enter the same email address you use to log into the program's member area. [L0517.001]
  3. Submit it so the membership can be verified and access granted. [L0517.001]
- A resources channel in the program Discord holds tools, supplier lists, ad creative examples and research templates. A Meta ads guide built for the Source D system sits alongside it. [L1452.011]

#### Facebook group as a working channel

- A Facebook group can serve as a working channel between mastermind calls for quick questions and answers, for sharing problems and asking for fast help. [L0832.039]
- The private, members-only Facebook group is used to post questions, share what is and is not working, and get quick help from the host and other members. Members can also ask the host directly there. (older: 2021-22) [L0545.010]

#### Coaching portal (Source B program) (older: 2021-22)

- Top menu: Dashboard (returns to the entry screen from any page), Group Coaching Schedule, Facebook Group, Coaching Recordings. Personal menu: My Courses (returns to the dashboard), Settings (basic platform changes), Log Out. [L0545.007]
- The Group Coaching Schedule is a Google Sheet showing the current calendar month's coaching times, with a registration link per call. It is updated on the first day of every calendar month. [L0545.008]
- Registration for a group coaching call in the schedule is required to attend, because the Zoom link for the call is only sent after you register. Registering is the only way to access a session. [L0545.009]
- Recordings of all group coaching sessions are available to every member, so missed sessions can be watched later. Recordings are usually uploaded within one to two days after the session. [L0545.011]
- Program video modules give step-by-step implementation guidance for action items assigned during coaching calls, enabling independent execution of recommendations. [L0035.008]
- Members who are stuck can email the program's support team at support@dropshipbreakthrough.com. Questions can also be posted in the members-only Facebook group, where other members can answer quickly. [L0545.019]

### Task boards and order flow

#### Trello

- Trello is used for order flows (from order through fulfilment, shipping and invoicing), so the status of any order is visible on one board. [L0337.028]
- Trello is also used for projects: a team member picks a card, works on it and moves it to completed, which removes status-check messages. The free version was enough for the example business. [L0337.028]
- Trello is a free task-management tool for to-dos for each business. It lists clients and prospects to contact and colour-codes progress: red for not done, yellow for in progress, green for done. [L0440.021]
- Each store or project gets its own Trello board. [L1431.011]
- Trello is used to manage tasks: each task is placed in a category list, assigned to a team member and given a deadline, so the team can complete it overnight for review. Sort tasks into lists that match your workflow before assigning them. [L1441.017]
- Trello is a visual board system for quotes, orders, projects and product uploads, with cards, lists, labels, comments, attachments and due dates. Due dates make cards turn red when overdue and can send email notifications. Cards can be dragged between lists to show progress. (older: 2021-22) [L1396.031]
- Set a due date on each follow-up or order card so the team gets notified before and when it is due. Overdue cards appear in red on the board. Enable email notifications so reminders reach you outside the board. (older: 2021-22) [L1396.032]
- Trello's free plan at the time of the lesson allowed five boards with a small team of about three members plus the owner. The speaker was unsure whether this was truly free, so check the current plan limits before relying on them. Trello and Asana were reported to cost about $10 per user per month with a minimum number of seats. (older: 2021-22) [L1396.033]
- Zapier can connect Trello to Shopify or other tools so that actions in one app trigger updates in another. It was not yet working reliably for the speaker, so connect it step by step and test each trigger. Automations of this kind were the next planned step after manual boards. (older: 2021-22) [L1396.034]
- Trello is a task board tool for organising projects and tracking work across a remote business. [L0926.004]
- Procedure to set up a store project board in Trello (Source C):
  1. Create a board in Trello for the store project. [L1431.012]
  2. Create a big task card, for example Store creation, and list the sub-tasks: create Shopify account, install apps, prepare payment providers, marketing, and product research. [L1431.012]
  3. Add labels such as Not done, In progress and Done to show progress, using the pen icon to set the label text and colour. [L1431.012]
  4. Give each card a deadline. [L1431.012]
  5. Open Share and enter the email address of the assistant to invite them to the board. [L1431.012]
  6. Open Members and select the assistant so everyone knows who owns which task. [L1431.012]
  7. Use the filter to show only the cards assigned to you. [L1431.012]
  8. Attach any PDF or product criteria to the card if needed. [L1431.012]
- Recommended board structure from a coaching program (older: 2021-22):

| Board | Lists or tabs | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| Corporate Information | Corporate, Manufacturers, Price List, Login Info, Shopify Training, SOP, PDF Files | Company information, one tab per manufacturer, login details, Shopify Help Center links, phone, chat and email templates, logos and quote templates |
| Pending Tasks and Quotes | Quote Request, Quote Received, Quote Send | One card per customer and item, moved as the quote progresses, with follow-up due dates |
| Order Processing | Order Sent, Backorder, Claims and Damages | One card per order, with due dates on backorders and claims; cancelled orders stay on the board |
| Projects | Collection / Product Page Content, Blogs / Buying Guides, Email Content, Social Media, Other Tasks | Weekly task cards with target keywords, word length and product or collection links |
| VA Product Upload | One card per supplier | Supplier name, description source, image source and pricing rule |
| White Glove Quotes | Separate quote workflow | Installation quotes kept apart from regular quotes, with follow-up with both the manufacturer and the customer |

[L1396.074]

#### Asana

- Asana is the core business tool for hiring, task management, reporting and team collaboration. Use it for the job application pipeline, candidate tracking, onboarding tasks, order tracking (each order = task), daily and weekly reporting, and automatic performance dashboards. [L0263.011]
- Asana cost: free initially, then $10 per user per month for premium features with reports and advanced automation. [L0263.011]
- Asana cost, conflicting source: the Reports tab is available in the paid plan only, at $10 per user per month. [L0263.027]
- Asana is used to track order status through fulfilment stages and assign tasks to team members. Orders appear as cards on a board with status columns. [L0230.005]
- Asana is a similar project board tool that also offers a list view for tasks and sorting by due date. Choose between Asana and Trello based on whether you want board-style moves or list views. Both can handle the same operational boards. (older: 2021-22) [L1396.035]
- Asana tasks support due dates and extra detail fields, and personal projects in Asana are free, so it can also serve as a personal to-do list. [L1473.004]
- Asana Reports tab: an automatic live dashboard shows task completion by person, open tasks remaining, and trend over time (4 weeks, 7 days, yesterday). Use it to see team performance and workload distribution at a glance. Available in the paid plan only ($10 per user per month). [L0263.027]

#### ClickUp and Basecamp

- ClickUp can serve as the central task manager, with one list per area of responsibility, a calendar view that shows upcoming deadlines and calls, and filters or grouping by priority to work through tasks in order. Notion is an alternative for to-do lists. Keep all open tasks in one system so they do not spread across notes and memory. [L1041.032]
- In a ClickUp task description, type / to insert tables, templates, bulleted lists, headings or embedded videos. Use this to keep the description as a dynamic page instead of plain text. [L1181.030]
- In ClickUp, open the Everything view and group tasks by Priority to see all open work across lists in priority order. Save this grouped view and use it whenever free time appears. [L1181.031]
- Basecamp is useful for breaking up tasks across many people with due dates, but it is not needed until the business has a decent-sized team. [L0835.123]

### Screen recording and walkthroughs

#### Loom
