- Loom is a screen recording tool that captures your screen and optionally your camera, then gives you a shareable link to the video. It is useful for quick walkthroughs or explainer videos for team members or customers. [L0629.001]
- Loom is a free screen recording tool available as a desktop app or browser extension. It records screen, microphone and camera for instructional videos. [L0276.036]
- Loom is a free Chrome extension for recording screen, voice and optional webcam to document a process such as uploading products, for training a virtual assistant. (older: 2021) [L0801.111]
- Loom records your screen and microphone so you can show a team member how to do a task. (older: 2021) [L0671.083, L0671.220]
- Loom (free version), Google Meet or QuickTime can record your screen with microphone audio so that you can document how to perform a task. [L0359.052]
- Loom is a free screen recording tool that gives a shareable link. Use it to record how a product should be uploaded so a freelancer can follow it. (older: 2020) [L0852.080]
- Loom is a screen recording tool that records the screen and voice, useful for making quick tutorial or explanation videos. (older: 2020) [L1087.004]
- Loom is a free Chrome extension that records the screen, optionally with a webcam and microphone. It produces a video link or embed code that can be placed in a step-by-step guide. (older: 2020) [L1088.065]
- Loom is a free screen recording tool that adds a webcam bubble. Short Loom videos can be used to answer customer or lead questions more personally than email text. [L0972.030]
- Loom is a free screen and video recording tool, available as a Chrome extension or as a desktop app from loom.com. It works well for answering questions with a short visual explanation instead of long text. [L1049.001]
- When starting a Loom recording, choose whether to share your screen, your webcam and your microphone. Recording starts with one click and stops with one click, and the length can be anything from under a minute to several minutes. [L1049.002]
- After you stop a Loom recording, Loom generates a unique loom.com link for that video. Paste this link into an email reply or a Discord post so the recipient can watch it. [L1049.003]
- Loom records the product upload process; the videos are then sent to Fiverr freelancers. (2025) [L0255.053]
- A short screen recording tool such as Loom can show a store funnel to an outside expert, who can spot obvious errors the owner missed. A five-minute walkthrough of the website and funnel is enough for this. [L0694.042]
- Loom works when the owner also wants to appear on camera. [L0337.008]

#### QuickTime

- On a Mac, QuickTime is a simple way to record a screen-based process for the team. Press Command + Shift + 5 to start screen recording. [L0337.008]
- Videos do not need to be polished; they only need to show the steps clearly. [L0337.008]

### Hiring and freelance talent

#### Hiring pipeline tools

- Tools for the hiring process: onlinejobs.ph (job posting), Asana (hiring pipeline and candidate tracking), Asana forms (application screening) and Vocaroo (audio recording submission from candidates). These automate filtering and reduce manual work while maintaining visibility. [L0263.017]
- Message-based interviews: Skype is the main chat platform, and you ask the candidate for their Skype ID. Slack can be used the same way by collecting the candidate's email and inviting them to a dedicated channel. (older: 2021-22) [L1055.015]

#### onlinejobs.ph

- onlinejobs.ph is a hiring platform for virtual assistants based in the Philippines, used to find and hire customer service and admin staff. [L0970.091]
- Online Jobs PH is the job site used to hire virtual assistants from the Philippines, which gives access to a large pool of experienced remote staff. (2021) [L1090.065]
- onlinejobs.ph requires a monthly subscription of about $49 or $69 to access its VA database and message candidates before hiring. It is known for finding long-term, career-focused VAs, including many with Shopify skills. [L0921.027]
- onlinejobs.ph is also used to find helpers for product uploads, and to hire hourly freelancers for product uploads and other tasks. (2022) [L0665.055, L0359.051]
- onlinejobs.ph is a job board used to hire freelance video editors who make product creatives. It was the source of the editor used in the test. [L1194.022]
- TimeProof on onlinejobs.ph is a free feature that records the VA's work session through screenshots taken roughly every 10 minutes. It lets you review what the VA did on the computer. [L0921.021]

#### Upwork

- Upwork and onlinejobs.ph are used to hire hourly freelancers for product uploads and other tasks. Fiverr is not recommended for product upload work. [L0359.051]
- Upwork is used to hire Shopify developers for custom product tools or small theme changes, and bidders are usually available quickly. (2021) [L0671.081, L0671.218]
- Upwork is the platform for one-off projects such as uploading products or hiring a virtual assistant. (2021) [L0834.055]
- Upwork is used to hire freelancers for Shopify developer work, product uploads and site design, while virtual assistants can be hired from online-jobs.ph. (2020) [L0852.081]
- Upwork lets you post a project and receive bids from writers and specialists. Run test projects with several writers before choosing one for ongoing work. (2021) [L1279.046]
- Upwork can be used to hire a site speed specialist. Post the job tagged with Shopify, which costs nothing to post, and judge candidates by reviews for Shopify and speed work. (2022) [L0963.052]
- Hourly or fixed prices for site speed work vary widely, from about 20 USD to several thousand. Price alone does not indicate quality, so reviews are the main criterion. (2022) [L0963.053]
- Upwork and Fiverr are freelance marketplaces where a store owner can find first virtual assistants. The rate of bad hires there is high, so check the assistant's track record and consider a trial period before committing. [L1432.044]

#### Fiverr

- Fiverr is pay-per-job, so a single task such as an upload or small edit can be bought without a subscription, with prices around $10 per task. Its freelancer pool covers a very wide range of skills, and quality varies strongly. [L0921.029]
- Fiverr is a marketplace for hiring freelancers, used to outsource store builds, product pages, or purchasing pre-warmed Facebook ad accounts. [L1212.040]
- Source positions on Fiverr differ. Fiverr is not recommended for product upload work [L0359.051], while a single upload or small edit can be bought there for about $10 per task [L0921.029]. Use Fiverr for one-off tasks with a checked result, and use Upwork or onlinejobs.ph for recurring upload work. [L0359.051, L0921.029]

#### StoreTasker

- StoreTasker (storetasker.com) is a marketplace for hiring developers to do custom Shopify work. For simpler needs, the Shopify App Store offers apps that can customise a store. (2021) [L0853.098]
- StoreTasker is a freelance marketplace for one-off technical Shopify modifications and coding tasks, when a change is beyond what you can do yourself. (2023) [L0855.113]
- StoreTasker.com is used for website development and code work. You post a job, developers submit bids, and you approve one or decline all. [L0921.030]
- StoreTasker is listed as a tool for this business setup. The source does not describe its function, so verify what it does before using it. (2024) [L1095.006]

#### Hiring a VA: hand-over

- Clarify upfront which tools or platforms are required for the agency to work with you. Determine whether they require access to proprietary software, dashboards or creative management systems, and ensure your data remains accessible to you if the relationship ends. [L0217.044]

### Virtual assistant tools

- Hootsuite lets a social media VA log into one dashboard and publish the same product visuals to all connected social media accounts. Giving the VA access only to Hootsuite keeps posting centralised and avoids sharing account logins. [L0921.035]
- Google Drive, Google Sheets and Asana together provide the order and task view for the VA, with customer messages kept in the help desk. [L1473.006, L0230.005]

### Monitoring and activity checks

- Hubstaff is a time tracking tool that shows mouse and keyboard activity and time spent. Normal product listing metrics: 70% mouse, 30% keyboard. Abnormal: 100% keyboard and 100% mouse with no output, which is likely fake time tracking or off-task behaviour. [L0263.028]
- Use Hubstaff to detect when someone fakes time tracking, for example by playing videos or using software to simulate activity. It is not used as a pay-per-second basis, but as activity validation. [L0263.028]

### Access, backups and handover

- Give the virtual assistant limited access to the Shopify admin panel, only to the parts needed to see order status and enter tracking information. Do not give full admin access, the same limited-access approach used for phone support staff. [L1430.005]
- Rewinds is a Shopify app for backing up your store data before granting contractors access. (2024) [L0276.037]

### Research VPNs

- A VPN can be used during market research to see the search results and shopping results for the United States, if Google shows results for your own country instead. Any reputable VPN works, free or paid. (2020) [L0468.084]
- A VPN or proxy set to another country shows Google search results as local shoppers see them. Use it to check foreign competitors and Shopping ads from outside that country. (2024) [L0806.108]
- A VPN lets you see Google Shopping results as if you were in another country, which helps with price research where Google Shopping is unavailable locally. Proton VPN is free or costs about $10 per month. (2021) [L0853.052]
- ExpressVPN and similar VPN services change the apparent country of the connection. Use them to see country-specific search results, such as when the Google Shopping tab is missing in the research country. (2022) [L0963.054]
- ExpressVPN is a tool for securely connecting to the internet when working remotely from different countries. [L0926.003]
- ExpressVPN can be used to view your Google Ads or store as shown in your target country when you are abroad, because geo-targeted ads will not display to a user outside the country. Use it carefully, because a VPN can trigger account security checks. [L1441.022]
- NordVPN was named as the VPN to use on a desktop to access a US-region TikTok Ads Manager from a UK location. Reports on this setup were mixed and described as prone to issues, so test it with a small budget before relying on it. [L1186.042]
- A VPN subscription can be used to log in with an IP from the target country, such as Australia, New Zealand, the UK or an EU country, before opening TikTok Ads Manager or TikTok Business Center. A yearly NordVPN plan was cited at about $1 to $2. Turn the VPN on before starting the session, not afterward. [L1377.060]
- A VPN can be used to set an Instagram account to a target country, for example the US, so organic reels reach that audience. [L1212.044]

## AI Assistants and Custom AI Workflows

### Choosing and starting with AI chat tools

- ChatGPT is the most widely used AI chat tool. It works well for writing copy, generating marketing ideas, explaining concepts and learning new topics, and it is very beginner friendly. [L0887.001]
- Claude is a strong AI chat tool for longer written content, more structured answers, analysing information, deep thinking and problem solving. Choose it when a task needs careful analysis or a clearly organised output. [L0887.002]
- One AI chat tool, either ChatGPT or Claude, is enough to start. Pick one and begin using it daily instead of overthinking the choice. [L0887.003]
- For long, structured prompts that must analyse customer reviews, Claude tends to outperform ChatGPT. When the output matters, run the same prompt and inputs in both tools and keep the better result. [L0887.004]
- Use AI chat as the first point of contact for any store, marketing or business question, before asking a community or coach. It is available around the clock and can be used whenever a problem appears. [L0887.005]
- ChatGPT and Gemini produce equivalent brand name suggestions for dropshipping stores. Choose by personal preference or current access, because output quality does not differ. [L0320.006]
- The free version of ChatGPT contains all functions needed for niche brainstorming, so no paid plan is required. It can expand a broad category into specific product ideas. [L1056.011]
- The free version of ChatGPT is used to create content outlines and bullet points, not finished articles. The output is a starting structure that a person writes out in their own words. [L1321.008]
- The operator in one lesson reported that ChatGPT is free to use and keeps earlier conversation context, so later requests can build on earlier topics. [L0440.022]
- ChatGPT Professional cost about 20 dollars per month at the time the lesson was recorded (2023). It gives access to browsing and plugins that can pull data such as Amazon reviews. Use the base version when you only need copy generated from your own inputs. [L0516.048]
- The free plan of Claude has a message limit that can cut off a long generation mid-output. This happened when an ebook HTML file was truncated. Retry later, or use a paid plan and generate a full ebook in several steps. [L0862.021]
- Gemini asks for a login before it imports files. Attach the competitor video and exported brand data from a logged-in account, because file upload in the chat does not work without it. [L0374.006]
- ChatGPT Playground at platform.openai.com/playground is a free-to-use alternative when regular ChatGPT sign-up is blocked. It handles the same text tasks: product descriptions, ad copy, ad strategies, and preset answers for live chat. [L1428.001]
- Chat-based research works best with product context. When asking about any product, describe your current work first (for example that you run a Shopify store) to get more relevant output. [L0972.034]

#### Claude chat setup

- On Windows, press Ctrl+V to paste a captured screenshot into the Claude chat. Ctrl+B switched back to the Claude window from the Kalodata tab. [L0354.030]
- The Claude chat used for product research ran on Opus 4.8 at High effort, shown in the model selector of the chat. [L0354.033]

### Prompting rules and inputs

#### Giving the AI the right context

- A large language model answers from its training material. Always state the exact sources, definitions and step-by-step method the AI should follow, because it otherwise falls back on safe, generic answers. [L1478.007, L1478.003]
- Give the model as much product information as possible before asking for specific outputs. [L0321.022]
- When asking AI for help with a store problem, always state the platform (for example Shopify), the theme you use (for example the Elixir theme), the exact element involved and what happens instead. Without this context the answer will be generic. [L0887.008]
- When a prompt asks for Amazon listing data, take the title and bullet points from the product's Amazon listing and collect 3-5 detailed real customer reviews. Output quality depends on these inputs, so do not skip them. [L0887.007]
- Standard ChatGPT ad writing needs the product facts, the problem, the feeling and the benefit supplied in the prompt. [L1427.064]
- Brand guidelines given to a chat AI: provide only the cover page as a screenshot and the colour palette, not the full PDF. The AI may not read PDFs visually well, and too much information can confuse it. [L0884.023]
- Do not upload more than two PDF pages to ChatGPT. Upload only what the AI needs to see, because large PDFs reduce the quality of its output. [L0884.024]

#### Working in threads

- ChatGPT keeps earlier messages in the same chat, so follow-up requests can refine earlier output without restating the product or business name. Keep one chat per topic so the context stays relevant. [L0440.067]
- ChatGPT keeps the conversation context, so follow-up requests such as making text funnier or more emotional work without repeating the product details. It is useful for copywriting, ad copy, headlines, product descriptions, bullet points, video captions, brand name ideas, and colour palettes or hex codes. [L0605.010]
- ChatGPT retains context throughout a thread, so you can refer to previous examples and ask for variations. [L0321.023]
- An AI chat assistant works well as a sounding board. Describe your current work and ask for feedback or ideas, including through phone voice mode. For each specific task, ask the AI to write a detailed prompt for you, then edit it. [L0972.105]

#### Reusable prompt patterns

- Use a short, concrete prompt that names the problem, the goal and the number of options you want, so the AI proposes several solutions instead of one guess. Pattern: state the problem, state the desired outcome, then ask for three options. Examples: "My export file opens with broken characters; I need a clean CSV for Excel; what are three ways to fix this?" and "My agent keeps skipping the scoring step; I need every candidate scored; what are three changes I could make to the instructions?" [L0366.017]
- Beginner prompt for a stuck problem, which asks for options and then for changes to agents.md so the problem does not recur:

```
I am a beginner and I am stuck on this problem: [describe the problem]. I need this solution: [describe the goal]. What are three options you could give me, with the pros and cons of each? Once I choose one, tell me which lines to add or change in agents.md so the same problem does not happen again.
```
[L0366.018]

- Regularly put these kinds of prompts to an AI tool and tailor the specifics to the product you sell:

```
Regularly put these kinds of prompts to an AI tool and tailor the specifics to the product you sell: ask it to break down a dropshipping idea for someone new to the field, ask it to draft a description for a given item, ask it for five marketing angles worth testing on that product, and ask it why your ads might not be turning visitors into buyers. The more of these questions you ask, the more helpful the AI becomes for your business.
```
[L0887.011]

- Using a ready-made prompt from the course library:
  1. Find the prompt that matches your task, for example the one for store building. [L0887.006]
  2. Copy the complete prompt, including all instructions and section names. [L0887.006]
  3. Paste it into ChatGPT or Claude as a new chat. [L0887.006]
  4. Fill every required input: the Amazon listing title and bullets, the product description and 3-5 detailed customer reviews. Add your brand name and product name where the prompt asks for them. [L0887.006, L0887.007]
  5. Press send and wait for the full output. [L0887.006]
  6. Use the sections in the order the prompt generates them. [L0887.006]

#### Checking generated prompts

- A generated prompt must make sense before it is run. Read it, check it against your brand and product, and fix anything unclear. If you are unsure how to phrase a prompt, ask Claude to draft it and review the draft instead of running it blindly. [L0794.042]
- Dictating your brand, product and process aloud and having Claude turn that into a prompt is faster than typing. Check that the resulting prompt matches your brand before running it. [L0794.043]

#### Structure for product page and ad prompts

- Structure for using ChatGPT to generate product page copy:

```
Structure for using ChatGPT to generate product page copy:
1. Provide product category and name.
2. List product benefits found from competitor research.
3. Define your role: "You are a professional copywriter."
4. Ask specific question: "Provide a one-line USP for this product that shows the benefit and differentiates from normal [alternative]." Then iterate on the response.
5. For bullet points: "Provide four benefit-driven bullet points (not features) describing what customers will experience when using this product."
6. For FAQ: "Generate four common questions and answers for our product."
7. For paragraphs: "Provide a two-to-three-line paragraph that describes [specific aspect] in a benefit-driven tone."
8. For rewrites using another product as reference: "I have this copy for another product [paste example]. Generate similar copy for our product [product name]." ChatGPT retains context throughout the thread, so you can refer to previous examples and ask for variations.
```
[L0321.023]

- Bottom-of-funnel ad prompts: ask Claude or ChatGPT for four testimonial, free gift or benefit BOF ad prompts, or for primary text variations based on your customer research. You can also analyse a competitor ad. The source states this takes about 20 minutes. [L0316.022]

```
Make me four [testimonial/free gift/benefit] bottom-of-funnel ad prompts for ChatGPT
Based on my customer research, make me primary text variations for a BOF ad.
What makes this ad work? Create a prompt so I can make similar ads for my niche.
```
[L0316.022]

- When giving a generated prompt to an AI, a prompt that asks for an urgency offer can be built from the product, price point and niche. [L1382.070]

### Product copy, descriptions and ads

#### Store copy

- Shopify Magic is a built-in AI tool available in all plans for rewriting product copy. Highlight text, click Shopify Magic, and select Rephrase, Extend or Simplify. [L0036.020]
- ChatGPT is used to generate product page copy, headlines, benefit points, FAQs and alternative wording for existing copy. [L0321.022]
- Claude is used to turn copied product data and screenshots into a product page template, product description, testimonials, FAQ and collapsible tab text. Sonnet 4.6 was the model in the lesson. Check every output by hand before publishing, because the model can state things that are not true. [L1188.022]
- ChatGPT is used to write copy and scripts for the agency and dropshipping business, including cold calling scripts, cold emails, ad copy, product descriptions, business names, colour combinations and blog posts. [L0440.022]
- Read any generated copy against your store's facts and offer details before publishing. [L1287.036]
- Google Docs is the editing stage for AI drafts. Insert a [Product Grid] placeholder where the grid will go, so the copy can be placed above or below it during editing. [L0883.029]
- Claude MCP can connect Claude to a Shopify store so it can read and write store data. It is used for bulk meta-field and internal link updates, but changes should be reviewed before they are applied. [L1269.037]

#### Demo and photo-based descriptions

- Demo product descriptions: paste the description prompt into ChatGPT, attach the AI-generated product image, and let the AI infer realistic product details from the visual before copying the completed description. [L0088.005]
- Enter the full Demo Product Description Prompt, which starts with `You are a professional e-commerce copywriter specializing in high conversion Shopify product pages for premium products`, into ChatGPT. Attach an image file you created in Gemini to generate a professional product description. [L0219.003]
- AI product descriptions from photos were not possible with the ChatGPT version used at the time, since image input was not allowed. The workaround is to describe the product in text: its features, materials and target customer. Then ask the AI to write the description aimed at that customer. [L0353.037]
- After an image is uploaded to ChatGPT, the image view shows Markup, Comment, Remove BG, Erase, Resize and Describe buttons. These make a small area change more precise than a text-only request. [L0333.024]

#### Ads, offers and strategy

- ChatGPT can write product descriptions, ad copy and ad strategy ideas for an ecommerce store. It is the main AI writing tool in the lesson, with OpenAI's Playground as an alternative interface. [L1416.001]
- An AI chat tool can suggest which urgency offer fits a specific product. Give it the product, price point and niche, ask for time-limited offers such as a free shipping window, and keep only the ideas that fit the product margin. [L1382.070, L1436.047]
- Connecting Claude to the Facebook ad account lets you ask which campaigns are currently above or below break-even ROAS. This is a quick way to check what is working without reviewing every campaign by hand. [L1476.045]
- ChatGPT Professional, with web browsing and a review-reading plugin, can pull customer reviews from a product listing to find audience language and objections. [L0524.061]

#### Custom store code

- ChatGPT can generate custom Shopify code, such as a custom section that adds AI-powered calculators or widgets, including store locators that search by ZIP code. Native Shopify does not include a ZIP code store locator. [L0472.042]
- GPT Engineer is an open-source AI project that can write full code bases from a prompt. AI tools can speed up building, but users still need development knowledge to make the output work properly. [L0709.035]
- AI assistants can build store features from plain text instructions. Someone with development knowledge must check and fix the results, because the code may not work correctly without it. [L0709.036]

### Images, voice and transcription

#### Image tools

- The image generation layer of the email stack uses Higgsfield or ChatGPT Image 2, and is optional. [L1361.001]
- Higgsfield is also listed among the connectors in Claude, next to Klaviyo, Slack, Google Drive and Brandfetch. [L1506.019]

#### Voice input

- The Chrome extension `Voice Control for ChatGPT` lets you press the space bar and speak your prompt instead of typing it. It speeds up long briefs, such as long product and customer descriptions. [L0516.049]
- A browser voice-input extension for ChatGPT lets you dictate a prompt with a hotkey instead of typing it. This speeds up back-and-forth iteration on ad copy when you supply product details. [L0524.070]
- Dispatch (Beta) connects Claude Cowork to the Claude mobile app, so a running content task can be checked and answered from a phone. Keep Claude open on the laptop while the task runs, check progress every few minutes on the phone, and return after 20 to 30 minutes to edit the finished articles. [L0883.027]

#### Transcription

- Grok (X/Twitter's free AI) is used to transcribe long videos and coaching call replays. It is faster and more reliable than ChatGPT for long-form transcription. [L0255.061]
- For long transcripts that ChatGPT repeatedly cut off, Grok (grok.com, free) completed the job in a single pass. Use it when you need a full, uninterrupted text conversion. [L0972.033]
- Otter.ai can convert a recorded video or call into a text transcript. Paste the transcript into an AI tool for summaries and search. [L0966.068]
- rev.com is a transcription service used for long recordings, priced at about $1.25 per minute. Dictation on your computer works for short texts, and a recording can be sent for longer material. Older advice from 2020. [L0852.079]

### Other AI writing and marketing tools

- copy.ai is an AI copywriting tool that scans the web for best-performing copywriting. It provides inspiration for writing ad copy and product descriptions. [L0206.007]
- Copy.ai is an AI writing tool used to generate ideas and draft product descriptions. It is not a substitute for editing and human copywriting. Older advice from 2021. [L0801.113]
- An AI product-description tool based on GPT-3 cost $97 per month and offered a seven-day free trial without a credit card. It produced a unique description in about five seconds per product. Older advice from 2020, so check current pricing. [L0468.085]
- An AI Tool for Product Descriptions and More was listed as a resource in the December 2020 coaching call for generating product copy. The lesson names it without naming the tool or describing its use. Older advice from 2020. [L0426.011]
- Grammarly, as a free Chrome plugin, checks grammar, repeated words and writing quality in real time as you compose emails to suppliers. Available at grammarly.com. [L0251.023]
- Grammarly flags written copy that can be stated more clearly or succinctly. Its paid version shows more suggestions than the free one. An AI can perform the same tone rewrite on request, so a separate grammar or tone-checking step is optional. [L0353.058]
- Compose.ai can draft marketing emails for an ecommerce store. It can also write texts and SEO blog articles. [L1416.003]
- An agent wrapper for ChatGPT (reworked.ai) can research a product from its website and write ad copy on its own. It needs a paid account, an OpenAI API key and billing details, and it charges per task. The lesson's example of 25 tasks cost about 14 cents. [L1427.066]

#### Setting up an agent-style ad writer

1. Get a paid account for the agent tool. [L1427.067]
2. Create an OpenAI API key. [L1427.067]
3. Add billing details, since each task is charged. [L1427.067]
4. Enter the API key into the agent tool. [L1427.067]
5. Give a goal, such as researching the product from its website and writing an ad with the problem-agitate-solution framework. [L1427.067]

#### Free tiers and recommended tools

- ChatGPT Playground was among four AI tools recommended in the lesson, with Runway ML, Compose.ai and DALL-E. All four were presented as free to use, though free tiers and access terms can change. Verify each platform before relying on it. [L1428.015]
- Workflow automation tools such as n8n or Claude Code connect AI assistants and platforms with other software. Not every software has an open API, so some connections need a different approach. [L0485.026]
- Zapier is an automation tool that can connect the AI workflow to other services. It is mentioned as a reason to consider a paid ChatGPT plan. [L0353.036]
- Zapier connects apps to automate workflows, such as moving generated copy into a spreadsheet. [L0524.061]
- Google Sheets can integrate with Claude Code via API, for example by pushing updated forecasts to new sheets. The integration is not yet fully reliable and may require setting up Google APIs manually. Expect this capability to improve significantly. [L0218.020]

### Claude desktop app, Cowork and Claude Code

#### Installing and plans

- The Claude desktop app bundles Chat, Cowork and Claude Code in one application. It is available for macOS and Windows (x64 and arm64), and the Claude mobile apps for iOS and Android can be paired with it. [L1506.004]
- Cowork and Claude Code on the Claude desktop app require the Claude Pro plan. [L1506.003]
- Sign up for Claude with a paid plan, which the stack recommends, before using Claude Cowork. Alternatives are ChatGPT Codex at chatgpt.com/codex and Claude Code at claude.com/claude-code. [L1361.017]
- Download Claude only from the official claude.com download page, because look-alike sites are used for phishing. On a Mac, use the Download for macOS button on that page. [L1506.005]
- Terminal is needed to install and run Claude Code and to run the Claude CLI. The check command `claude -version` confirms the installation. [L1361.008]
- Claude Cowork is the native desktop app, which the source calls Cord. Use the desktop app, not the web browser version, to access Opus 4.6 and the large context windows needed for P&L processing. [L0218.004]
- Watch the complete walkthrough once before setting up anything in this email workflow. Then reread the written instructions and follow them step by step in order. [L1506.025]

#### Cowork

- Claude Cowork is a project management interface within Claude. You create dedicated projects, manage file folders, set model preferences (for example Opus 4.7), and invoke MCPs to gather and process brand data. [L0318.001]
- Model choices recorded across the courses differ by date, so use the model the current interface offers:
  - Opus 4.6 for Cowork processing of P&L files. [L0218.004]
  - Opus 4.7 for Brandfetch MCP tasks, selected because it was the most powerful model available at the time. [L0318.007, L0318.001]
  - Opus 4.8 High shown in the project model selector for the email template workflow. [L1506.028]
- When invoking an MCP tool such as Brandfetch, the `act without asking` toggle controls warnings and confirmations. With it disabled, all warnings are shown and Claude pauses for review. With it enabled, the tool runs immediately without stopping. [L0318.018]
- Approval mode is chosen per project under the model selector. `Ask before acting` makes Claude pause for approval of each action. `Act without asking` lets Claude work without pausing, which is the mode used for the email template setup. [L1506.011, L1506.028]
- Leave `Act without asking` switched off for Chrome, because the email template workflow does not need browser control. [L1506.012]
- The project page has a `Set up recurring tasks` option, which schedules repeated work for that project. [L1506.029]
- After a skill is started from the slash menu, Claude asks follow-up questions for each input it needs and then builds the templates. The user does not need to type an extra prompt. [L1506.031]
- If Cowork blocks some commands, open the Claude Code terminal and run the command there. This is the fallback for commands Cowork refuses to execute. [L1506.024]

#### Claude Code and VS Code

- Visual Studio Code is a free IDE that integrates Claude Code for advanced financial forecasting beyond what Cowork can handle. [L0218.005]
- Claude Code uses a `claude.md` file, where Codex uses an `agents.md` file, for the same purpose: standing instructions for a folder. Both work in a similar way. [L0366.002]
- Cowork and ChatGPT Codex perform at roughly the same level for the email workflow, so the choice comes down to personal comfort. Claude Code is an acceptable alternative for users who already work in it. [L1361.002]
- Claude Code file management: use Python to read and write xlsx and csv files directly from the project folder instead of asking the user to copy and paste. When new files appear in the folder, immediately identify them, add them to DATA_INVENTORY.md, and surface the top 3 insights. [L0218.017]

Setup procedure for Claude Code in VS Code:

1. Download Visual Studio Code (free). [L0218.008]
2. Open Extensions and search for `Claude`. [L0218.008]
3. Install Claude for VS Code by Anthropic. [L0218.008]
4. Click Trust Publisher to allow the extension. [L0218.008]
5. Open the Claude website to authorise your subscription. [L0218.008]
6. Create a new folder named `Claude_CFO` (no symbols). [L0218.008]
7. Open the folder in VS Code and select Trust. [L0218.008]
8. Drag the CFO operating prompt MD file into the project folder. [L0218.008]

### Connectors and integrations (MCP)

- A connector is the name for an MCP (Model Context Protocol) integration that lets Claude read and write to a software tool. Connectors are managed in `Customize > Connectors`. [L1506.018]
- To add a custom connector, go to `Customize > Connectors` and choose to add a custom connector. Enter the name and details given in the course. The connector then appears in the list, for example Klaviyo, Slack, Google Drive, Higgsfield and Brandfetch. [L1506.019]
- Connector tool permissions are set per connector with `Always allow`. The Brandfetch connector offers read-only tools such as Search Brands, Build Logo URLs, Get Brand Details and Get Brand Context, which are set to `Always allow`. [L1506.020]
- In `Customize > Connectors`, Klaviyo is listed under a `Not connected` heading until it is linked. Check this status before starting any email work. [L1506.032]

#### Brandfetch

- Brandfetch MCP scrapes a brand's own website and pulls its brand voice, colours, fonts and other identity elements into a brand kit. The kit is then used to style the universal content blocks. [L1361.003]
- Brandfetch requires a developer account at the Brandfetch developer portal (brandfetch.com), where a private API key and client ID are generated. The MCP is installed with that key, and the Pro plan is recommended for the sign-up. [L1361.006]

#### Klaviyo

- Klaviyo MCP connects the AI tool to a Klaviyo account, so the AI can read existing data such as campaigns and subscriber information, and write new email content back into Klaviyo. [L1361.004]
- Klaviyo requires a private API key generated in the Klaviyo developer portal (developer.klaviyo.com) to connect the AI tool. [L1361.007]
- Do not install the Klaviyo MCP connector for the template workflow. The source calls it flawed and very limiting, and says it does not allow the template and content block work that is needed. [L1506.015]
- Connect Claude to Klaviyo directly through the Klaviyo API, using the Python scripts supplied inside the skills, rather than through the Klaviyo MCP. The API connection is significantly more capable and covers the full template workflow. [L1506.016]
- The older setup check still lists the Klaviyo MCP as one of its three checks, so when the two disagree, follow the template-workflow instruction for Claude desktop: use the API. [L1361.005, L1506.015, L1506.016]

### Custom AI assistants and Claude projects

#### What a custom assistant is

- A custom AI assistant is a general LLM such as Claude or ChatGPT loaded with your own instructions, product information, templates and step-by-step processes. At minimum it can read and produce any text task. Visuals depend on the platform and LLM used. [L0485.017]
- In Claude, a project holds the assistant's instructions and reference files. The instructions specify how to create particular ad types or how to research ideas, and the files provide brand-specific information. [L0485.018]
- A custom assistant's output quality depends on the instructions: a large body of detailed instructions, based on a proven system, keeps the output on the right path. The assistant then produces research documents, scripts and visual assets from the information it was given. [L0485.021]
- A generic LLM without brand-specific instructions and files produces generic research based on general sources such as platform guidelines or old top search results. This dilutes the messaging and can lead to a poor creative path. [L0485.020]
- Example kickoff prompt for a market study. Before sending it, upload the product description and the brand's internal strategy documents into the assistant's workspace, so the study follows your own methodology rather than generic sources. Specify that the results arrive in the layout your templates prescribe.

```
Hello Magnus. First go through the attached product description, and afterwards run a market study for our label.
```
[L0485.019]

#### Files and capacity

- Reference files for a Claude project can be uploaded as Markdown (.md) or Word (.docx) files. The format affects how easily the assistant reads them. Markdown is generally easier for AI to parse, so mixing formats is workable, but plain-text files are the better default. [L0485.032]
- The Files panel of a Claude project shows a capacity indicator with the percentage of project capacity in use. In the example, about ten brand files used 15% of capacity. [L0485.033]
- For the script prompt to produce a usable script, add five reference files to the Claude project: a voice guide, a structure library, a banned word list, an avatar, and a psychology lens. Without these files the script stays generic and must be checked by hand. [L1140.028]

#### Building workflows with AI

1. Describe the outcome you want in plain language. [L0485.027]
2. Ask Claude to design the workflow that achieves it. [L0485.027]
3. Ask Claude to build it in the workflow tool or as code. [L0485.027]
4. Check the result manually for mistakes and holes in the process before relying on it. [L0485.027]

### Email AI stack (Source E)

- The AI email stack in this course has four layers:
  - AI builders for planning and template work: Claude Cowork as the main tool, ChatGPT Codex as an equal alternative, and Claude Code only if preferred. [L1361.001]
  - An image generation layer, Higgsfield or ChatGPT Image 2, which is optional. [L1361.001]
  - A brand identity layer: Brandfetch MCP extracts brand voice, colours and fonts from a website. [L1361.001]
  - The email platform Klaviyo, connected through the Klaviyo MCP (or, for the template workflow, the API) so data can be read and written between the AI tool and the account. [L1361.001, L1506.016]
- The email stack's skills run as slash commands inside Claude Cowork:
  - `/email-template-builder` for reusable blocks. [L1361.018]
  - The email flow builder skill for flows. [L1361.018]
  - `/email-draft-review` for draft checks. [L1361.018]
  - `/email-domain-audit` for deliverability. [L1361.018]
  - `/email-strat-plan` for campaign planning. [L1361.018]
- Foundation stage: install every tool from the course bundle zip and set up a working stack with a defined folder structure before any email content is built. Nothing else should start until this stage is complete. [L1361.019]

Setup verification for the AI email stack in Claude Cowork:

1. Ask Claude Cowork to list your Klaviyo campaigns through the Klaviyo MCP. [L1361.005]
2. Ask it to look up your brand domain with Brandfetch. [L1361.005]
3. Type `/email-template-builder` to confirm the template skill is available. [L1361.005]

All three checks should respond. If one fails, fix the corresponding tool or connection before continuing. [L1361.005]

### AI agent folders (Codex and Claude Code)

#### Concepts and set-up

- Codex is a desktop app from ChatGPT that can run AI agent folders on your computer. As of May 2026 it was recommended for beginners over Claude Code, because you link your existing ChatGPT account and set-up is simpler. [L0366.001]
- Codex and Claude Code can be compared as follows. Claude Code is the folder-instruction tool with claude.md, and Codex uses agents.md. For the email workflow, the two are comparable in level. [L0366.002, L1361.002]
- The agents.md file is the most important file in an agent folder. It holds the standing instructions that shape how the AI behaves in that folder. [L0366.003]
- Agent folders can be downloaded from GitHub, which also makes it possible to deploy and update them later. A downloaded folder is then opened locally in Codex. [L0366.004]
- Create one dedicated AI folder on your computer and place agent folders inside it. Anything dragged into that folder structure is visible to the AI, so keep sensitive data such as credit card details out of it. [L0366.005]
- Keep one master AI folder on your computer and place each agent folder inside it as a subfolder. Open each agent folder separately in Codex, because each opening starts its own chat scoped to that folder. [L0366.027]
- To connect an agent folder, open Codex and go to `File > Open folder`, then select the agent folder. Codex starts a new chat scoped to that folder. [L0366.007]
- In the Codex chat window, the access setting is at the bottom and set to `Full access` for this agent, so the agent can run without asking for each action. The model is set to 5.5 with reasoning effort at Extra High, shown in the same selector. [L0366.008]
- In the Codex chat composer there is a `Toggle /Fast` option that makes responses faster. Codex may suggest enabling it based on past usage, and it increases plan usage. Keep Extra High reasoning for careful agent work where depth matters more than speed. [L0366.025]
- Review and Undo: after an agent edits files, Codex shows a count such as `Edited 3 files` with Undo and Review buttons. Review lists each changed file with its line changes, for example +9 -2 for nine lines added and two removed. Check every change before keeping it, and use Undo to revert. [L0366.009, L0366.026]

First start of a downloaded agent folder:

1. Download the agent folder, for example from GitHub, and place it inside your dedicated AI folder. [L0366.010]
2. Open Codex and go to `File > Open folder`, then select the agent folder. [L0366.010]
3. Set access to `Full access` and the model to 5.5 with Extra High reasoning. [L0366.010]
4. Start a new chat and send the security-check prompt before anything else runs. [L0366.010, L0366.011]
5. Open the handoff document and the startup prompt file yourself and read them, if you want to see them directly. [L0366.010]
6. Read the agent's explanation of what will happen and check that it matches what you want. [L0366.010]
7. Click go to load the agent and start its workflow. [L0366.010]
8. Answer the setup questions the agent asks before it begins searching. [L0366.010, L0366.013]

Security-check prompt:

```
Please explain what is inside this folder and what it is for. It is a project I want to start working on. Check whether anything in it is malicious or could harm my computer or my data. Then explain step by step what will happen when we start, beginning with an analysis of the handoff document and the startup prompt, so I know what I am agreeing to before anything runs. Do not change, send, or delete any files yet.
```
[L0366.011]

- Running an unfamiliar agent folder with Full access before checking its contents for malicious instructions or files is risky. Always read the handoff and startup files and ask the AI to explain the workflow first. [L0366.012]

#### Setup questions and what agents do

- The agent's setup questions show what it needs before it searches: the target role or candidate type, what a strong fit and a disqualifier look like, where proof of work can be found, and any example people, companies, websites or keywords to start from. Clear answers make the first pass more useful. [L0366.013]
- Recruiting-style agents are built to search public sources only, unless you explicitly approve something else. They add qualified candidates to a tracking CSV with evidence, scores, rankings, risks and next steps. [L0366.014]
- The agent must not contact anyone, submit forms, share lists, change permissions or update outside systems unless you explicitly approve that exact action. Ask for this approval in writing for each external step. [L0366.015]
- Scope file searches and reads to the agent folder unless you explicitly ask for a wider search. Writing this rule into agents.md keeps the agent from browsing unrelated files on your computer. [L0366.024]

Files in a recruiting agent folder:

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| AGENTS.md | Standing instructions for the agent: rules, boundaries and how to behave in this folder |
| START-HERE-PROMPT.md | Startup prompt that tells the agent how to begin the workflow |
| HANDOFF.md | Beginner-facing setup and execution guide for the agent kit |
| growth-mentor-sourcing.md | Role-specific sourcing playbook with candidate qualification rules |
| role-sourcing-template.md | Copyable template for creating a new role-specific sourcing playbook |
| growth-mentor-candidates.csv | Working candidate tracker, where qualified people are logged |
| starter-candidates.csv | Starting list of candidates the agent works through first |

[L0366.016]

#### Getting unstuck and improving agents

- When an agent keeps giving wrong answers or misunderstands the task, the fix is usually a change to agents.md rather than repeated prompting. Asking the agent to update agents.md after each repeated error makes its later responses more accurate. [L0366.020]
- Standing safety prompt for a beginner, which also makes the agent keep agents.md up to date:

```
I am a beginner. Please always run the security checks before doing any work in this folder, and explain each step in simple language. Add these instructions to agents.md so you follow them in future sessions. Update agents.md whenever the same mistake repeats or when we learn something new in this process.
```
[L0366.019]

- When you get stuck, ask the AI first, for example when a folder asks for an integration that does not exist, before asking a mentor. Agents can usually propose a workaround or a custom tool. [L0366.021]
- Example: an agent may report that it cannot push files to Google Drive because no Google Drive integration exists. Ask the AI how to work around this, for example by saving a local file that is then uploaded manually or by building a small custom tool. [L0366.022]
- An agent folder can be used for YouTube scripts, creative ideas and recruiting. A recruiting scraper can search LinkedIn, YouTube and Twitter for potential clients. Any agent can be used for a new project, provided its instructions are set up correctly. [L0366.023]

### AI limits and verification

#### How AI produces answers

- A large language model such as ChatGPT or Claude predicts the most useful next output based on the text it was trained on. It does not produce original insight from nothing, so its answers are only as good as its source material. [L1478.002]
- AI output quality depends entirely on the training material it is given. If the sources are wrong, outdated or unproven, the answers will be unreliable even when they sound confident. [L1478.003]
- When a general AI model draws on many conflicting marketing opinions, it averages them into a watered-down answer that commits to no clear path. Broad questions without a defined source for marketing topics produce this diluted result. [L1478.004]
- Because AI is trained to avoid mistakes, it falls back on safe, generic answers when it is unsure which source is best. Always state the exact sources, definitions and step-by-step method the AI should follow instead of leaving this open. [L1478.007]
- A single trained person can only do one task at a time, whereas trained AI agents can apply the same proven instructions across many tasks at once. This is the main reason to build a repeatable, well-instructed system for AI. [L1478.010]
- Generative AI works by predictive generation: the model outputs what would be most useful to say next, based on the question and everything it was trained on. Terms such as predictive modelling describe the same process, so a different label does not change how the answer is produced. [L1478.013]
- Large language models such as ChatGPT and Claude are trained on mixed text sources, including books, blogs, essays, forum and social media posts, Wikipedia, and other web pages. Their answers reflect that mix, so topics with conflicting opinions, like marketing, produce inconsistent results. [L1478.014]
- A model trained only on a 300-year-old science book can answer questions from that book well, but it cannot propose new experiments or ideas beyond what the book contains. Its output is limited to the scope of its training material. [L1478.015]
- Chat assistants perform well on general questions largely because they can draw on the wider internet. Broad access to sources does not mean the answer is proven for a specific business task, so the source material for that task still needs to be chosen deliberately. [L1478.016]

#### Verification

- Every generated list, email or statement must be checked before publishing. AI tools such as ChatGPT and Gemini sometimes produce incorrect answers. [L1287.036]
- ChatGPT is not reliable for current trend data, such as lists of the top dropshipping products for the coming period or exact market numbers. Verify any figures it gives before using them, and use it mainly for text and ideas. [L0605.011]
- Many prepackaged AI dropshipping tools are thin wrappers around general chat models such as ChatGPT, Claude, Copilot or Grok, and are often built on outdated technology. Using the general models directly is usually cheaper and more current. [L0972.104]
- Do not use an unfamiliar agent folder with Full access before checking its contents. [L0366.012]

#### Changing features and limits

- ChatGPT's web browsing was switched off temporarily after users found ways to bypass privacy controls through links. AI tool features such as web search can be removed or changed without notice, so check current availability before building a workflow on them. [L0353.060]
- Pro plans have character limits, so long documents may need to be shortened. [L1427.064]
- The four AI tools recommended in this lesson (ChatGPT Playground, Runway ML, Compose.ai and DALL-E) were presented as free to use. Free tiers and access terms can change, so verify each platform before relying on it. [L1428.015]
- Free-plan limits and file import rules, such as the Gemini login requirement and the Claude free-plan message cap, change over time. Check them on the platform before planning a large job around them. [L0374.006, L0862.021]

#### Security and sources

- Download AI apps only from the official site. Look-alike sites are used for phishing. [L1506.005]
- Scope agent access to the folder you opened, and keep sensitive data out of any folder the AI can read. [L0366.005, L0366.024]

#### Further details

Quick comparison of the two recommended AI chat tools for beginners:

| Feature | ChatGPT | Claude |
|---|---|---|
| Ease of use | Very beginner friendly | Also simple |
| Best for | Marketing, ideas, copywriting | Deep thinking and structured answers |

Both tools are strong enough for store work, so the choice matters less than starting to use one daily.
[L0887.030]

## Product, Competitor and Ad Research Tools

### Choosing tools and limiting spend

- Pay for a research tool for about one month, then cancel if it does not produce useful products in that time. Use a strict rule: invest in one or two tools to find around 10 to 50 products in a month. [L0335.023]
- Start with only the one or two research tools you will use every week, because each subscription is a fixed monthly cost that must be covered before any sales arrive. [L1387.007]
- An ad spy tool is only worth its cost when you have a specific product or niche in mind, such as checking which shoes, slippers or bags perform best in one market before selling in another. For general product research without a clear goal, it is not worth the expense. [L1017.025]
- Research and spy tools often cost $15 to $99 per month each, and some cost more, such as Pipiads or PPSpy at about $263 and $299 per month on the list prices shown. A new store should budget these as fixed monthly costs before it has revenue to cover them. [L1387.001]
- Pick one spy tool and use it consistently for product searches and keyword checks. The lesson names Dropispy, Peeksta, Adspy and Minea as options. [L0636.004]
- Saturation-checking tools can help screen competition, but they do not replace a small paid test. The Facebook data from your own ads decides whether a product works. [L1161.052]
- Some sellers hide their store from spy tools by paying the tool operators to remove them, or by adding code to the store that blocks scraping. The mentor reported these methods but has not tested them. [L0335.047]

### Niche validation and market data

- The Source A Research Tool provides real data from trusted sources for niche research and saves hundreds of hours of manual research. Accessed via the online platform, it displays grade, average price, demographic appeal, brand loyalty, seasonality and demand metrics for any niche you enter. Use it for all market data in your niche selection process. [L0045.006]
- The Source A research software has unlimited use and returns a niche's monthly Google search volume, seasonality verdict and average price analysis within seconds. It can test any number of niches, not only a short shortlist. [L0451.015]
- Set up the research workspace with the worksheet on the left half of the screen and the research tool on the right half in split-screen view, or open the tool in its own browser tab. Copy each niche keyword from the worksheet and paste it into the tool without switching back and forth. [L1081.022]
- Source A Research Software is a Source A research tool named in an October 2025 coaching call. The document only lists it and does not describe its features. [L1106.003]
- Research tools for members are at the source's own site and use the same login as the blueprint course. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0969.055]
- Niche Verification is a tool for checking whether a niche is worth pursuing before committing money and time to it. [L0851.003]
- Google Trends is the primary tool for checking demand and seasonality for a product. Set the region and a five-year time range to see whether demand is steady, spiky or truly seasonal. (Older advice, 2021.) [L1090.062]
- The Fast Business Forum (fastbusinessforum.com) is used to search for niche selection advice from other sellers. You need to be logged in to post, and private messages are sent by clicking a member's name. (Older advice, 2018.) [L1085.047]

### Free ad libraries

#### Meta Ad Library and Facebook Ad Library

- The Meta Ad Library is a free tool that shows active ads from advertisers, including how many ads a creative runs in. Use it to study competitors, find creatives that run in many ads and check which hooks big brands keep using. [L0398.052]
- The Meta Ad Library shows every active ad a brand runs, with start date, format and copy. It is reached from a competitor's page under Page transparency, and the country can be changed to show regional ads. [L0649.057]
- The Meta Ad Library is a free tool that shows all running ads. Filter by country, search by keyword or brand, and screenshot ads for inspiration. [L0972.031]
- The Meta Ad Library is a free, public Meta database where you can search currently running ads by keyword and see which pages and stores run them. Use it to find competitor stores through the ads they run. [L1249.016]
- The Facebook Ad Library lets you search by country, category and keyword or advertiser name to see the ads a competitor is running on Facebook. [L0855.115]
- Check competitor ads for the same niche title in the TikTok Ads Library and the Facebook Ads Library as well as the Meta Ad Library. Bookmark them next to Kalodata for quick access. [L0354.032]
- Use the Ads Library filters active ads, sort by active ads and see summary details. Use the link from each ad to check the store and its other ads. [L1173.017]
- AdLib integrates with the Facebook Ad Library. Click download in AdLib to save videos and thumbnails directly, or use the Facebook Ad Library link for manual downloads. [L0209.041]
- Winning Hunter offers a free Chrome extension that works with the Meta Ad Library. Install it when you want to pull ads from the Meta Ad Library into Winning Hunter's analysis views. [L0772.039]

#### Ad spy tools in general

- Spy tools track competitor ads and creatives, showing which ads are running for big brands. Use them to find the creatives a niche is scaling before you test similar hooks. [L0398.055]
- Ad spy tools such as Pinterest, Snapchat and TikTok searches can show winning products. The speaker runs Facebook and TikTok side by side to see which platform works better for a product. [L0690.029]
- Spy tools such as Minea and Dropispy show ads and competitor products across platforms and are used for product research. [L0788.044]
- Spy tools show what competitors advertise and which products they sell. Tools such as Foreplay, Adspy and Dropispy are used for this kind of ad and product research. [L1387.012]

### Paid Meta and Facebook ad spy tools

#### AfterLive

- AfterLive builds extra filters on top of the Meta Ad Library data, such as active ads count, country, platform and a 30-day performance view. The mentor considered it the best tool for finding recent ads on Meta. It starts at about $50 to $80 per month, with a free trial. Other spy tools mostly show older ads, and some sellers can block them. [L0335.008]
- The source names this tool Afterlip, with a $9 per month plan, down from $99 per month. Its features may differ from the full plan shown in the tutorial, so compare the plan's feature list with the tutorial before assuming the same functions are available. [L0349.038]
- AfterLive setup: Technology set to Shopify; countries set to United States, United Kingdom, Canada and Australia; call-to-action set to Shop Now or Learn More; Active Ads minimum set to 10; Performance filter over the last 30 days. [L0335.014]
- The Performance view shows a progression of new active ads per day over the chosen period, so you can see a competitor's launch pattern. The Trending view highlights competitors scaling aggressively in a shorter time. A 7-day data option is available for very recent winners. [L0335.015]

#### DropiSpy

- DropiSpy research setup: date filter set to the last one month; likes, shares and comments thresholds; e-com platform set to Shopify; call-to-action set to Shop Now or Learn More; countries set to United States, Canada, Australia and United Kingdom. [L0335.013]
- DropiSpy costs about $29 per month at list price, or about $15 to $20 per month with a discount code. Rental platforms that resell tool logins sometimes list it for about $2 to $10 per month. [L0335.022]
- The bundle price capture lists Dropispy at $249 per month. The two figures conflict; the $29 list price applies to the provider's own plan, while $249 is the bundle's list price. Check the current price on the provider's site before buying. [L0335.022, L1387.003]

#### AdSpy

- AdSpy is a paid Facebook ad spy tool costing $150 per month. It lets you search by keyword, filter by a date range such as ads started since January 1, and sort ads by likes or shares to spot trends. [L0388.017]
- AdSpy is an ad library for searching competitor ads by keyword, age, gender, country and store URL. A free account gives up to 20 pages of search per day. [L0661.044]
- If AdSpy asks you to buy an account, do not pay for it. Create a new account and use the free search. If your email was used on an earlier account, sign up with a new email address. [L0661.045]
- AdSpy finds older Facebook ads, such as those from 2019 and 2020. It was considered expensive, so compare its monthly cost with alternatives such as Peeksta. [L0706.039]

#### Peeksta

- Peeksta (peeksta.com) and adtargeting.io help find niche-specific Meta interests for single-interest test ad sets. [L0376.007]
- Peeksta is an ad spy tool used to find older Facebook ads, such as those from 2019 and 2020. It was presented as a cheaper alternative to Adspy, at about $50 per month. The price figures in the transcript are inconsistent, so check the current price before subscribing. [L0706.038]
- Peeksta is used for product research, to find products that are performing well. It is a paid-style research tool referenced for locating winning products. [L1367.003]

#### AfterLib

- AfterLib is a paid product research tool, costing about $150 per month, that ranks products by performance or by number of active ads. It is used to build the first candidate list before checking each product manually in the Facebook Ad Library. [L1172.003]
- AfterLib is a paid ad spy tool recommended for its large library and data on how competitors scale their ads. It shows an ad's progression, meaning the growing number of ads per creative over time, which signals a profitable product. [L1174.021]
- AfterLib shows how the ad activity of a product changes over time. Use it to spot products whose ads have risen by roughly 100 to 300 percent in the last 14 days. [L1215.039]
- AfterLib is considered expensive compared with Peeksta, but it offers a free trial of about 7 to 14 days. Its data is pulled from the Ad Library itself, and experienced sellers consider its sales tracker accurate. [L1177.051]
- AfterLib sign-up accepts a public discount code, SAVE10, for 10 percent off. Sellers who want 25 to 30 percent can ask the vendor's support team directly for a larger discount. [L1177.052]
- AfterLib shared-access membership: a team plan allows several people to log in to one AfterLib account. The reported price for one person's shared access is $6.99. [L1216.026]

AfterLib filter setup:

1. Click All filters. [L1174.022]
2. Under Technology, choose Shopify. [L1174.022]
3. Under Country, select the target country; for a general setup use United States, United Kingdom, Canada, New Zealand and Australia. [L1174.022]
4. Under Language, choose English. [L1174.022]
5. Under Call to action, choose Shop now and Learn more. [L1174.022]
6. Under Creation date, prefer the last 30 to 45 days to find recently launched ads (results are already sorted by performance, so this step is less critical). [L1174.022]

AfterLib steps to reach the competitor's Facebook Ad Library page:

1. Click the three dots next to the product result. [L1174.023]
2. Click Go to meta page to open the competitor's Facebook Ad Library page. [L1174.023]
3. Apply the Active ads filter. [L1174.023]
4. Count the active ads for the product only. [L1174.023]
5. Subtract any active ads that belong to other products the competitor is testing on the same page, then record the adjusted number. [L1174.023]

#### Mynia

- Mynia is an alternative ad spy tool with data for Facebook, Pinterest and TikTok. It shows likes, comments, shares, page creation date, the audience targeted and other competitors fetched for the same search. [L1174.026]
- Mynia combines Pinterest, Facebook and TikTok ads in one place. It is used to spot new products and see which competitors run ads across platforms. [L1377.032]

Mynia filter setup for Facebook product research:

1. Log in and open the ad section, then select Facebook. [L1174.025]
2. Under date, choose the creation date filter for the last 30 days. [L1174.025]
3. Under CTA, choose Buy now, Shop now and Learn more. [L1174.025]
4. Under ecommerce platform, choose Shopify. [L1174.025]
5. Under language, choose English. [L1174.025]
6. Set engagement minimums: at least 200 likes, at least 100 comments and at least 100 shares. [L1174.025]
7. Select countries: United States, United Kingdom, Australia and Canada. [L1174.025]

Mynia has no direct link to the competitor's Ad Library, so use this path:

1. Open the ad post in Mynia. [L1174.027]
2. Click the page name. [L1174.027]
3. Open the About section and then Page transparency. [L1174.027]
4. Click See all and scroll down to Go to ad library. [L1174.027]
5. On the Ad Library page, click active ads and count the ads for the specific product. [L1174.027]

### Winning Hunter

- Winning Hunter is the AI product research tool recommended for finding candidate products. It is preferred over Kalodata, which was used earlier. [L0491.016]
- Winning Hunter is preferred for product research, but Kalodata remains useful as one signal source alongside TikTok Shop and competitor stores. [L0491.017]
- The Brands tracker shows one overview of a Meta advertiser: top ad copies, headlines, hooks, landing pages, personas, concepts, angles, desires, emotions, awareness stages and USPs, pulled from live ads by default. Each brand can be set to track Global Ads or EU/UK-only ads. [L0374.001]
- Newly added brands can take hours before all sections of the overview are filled in. [L0772.029]
- The brand tracker covers Meta advertisers only, and each brand page links to the same ads in the Meta Ad Library. Check the ads directly in the Ad Library before relying on the tracker data. [L0374.005]
- Each breakdown tab in a brand's overview (ad copies, headlines, hooks, landing pages, personas, concepts, angles, desires, emotions, awareness stages, USPs) offers time filters 7D, 30D, 3M, 6M, Custom and All, plus an Export CSV button. Sort options include Usage and longest running. [L0374.003]
- Winning Hunter lets you export a brand's top hooks, personas and angles. Feeding that export into Claude AI can uncover additional personas and angles. [L0772.030]
- Quick actions for a brand: open Brands, select the brand and use the Quick Actions menu. Track brand follows the advertiser, and Track store does the same for a Shopify store. Visit Product Page opens the store. Download Video and Download Thumbnail save creative files. Transcribe ad with AI produces a script of the ad. Other listed actions include Page Ad Library and Fulfill with Ecommerce. [L0374.002, L0772.031]
- The ad details panel shows running days, the number of duplicates (other advertisers using the same creative), the landing page URL, the product price, estimated monthly traffic and the first and last seen dates. Use these fields to judge how long and how widely an ad has run. [L0374.004]
- Winning Hunter's Ad Library filter limits results to one ad per brand by default, so you see fewer winning ads than exist. Turn that limit off when you want the full set of matches for a niche search. The limit is currently active, so expect smaller result counts when scanning. [L0374.040, L0772.015]
- The Ad Library search in the brand tool has a source dropdown with Meta Ads Library (marked recommended), Facebook Adspy, Pinterest Ads and TikTok Ads. Start with Meta Ads Library when you need ads that run on Meta. [L0374.042]
- The Ad Library filter panel has a Save current filters button, so a niche search can be reused without setting every field again. Preset shortcuts also exist for Week's biggest winners, European winners, US winners, Dropship Ads, Advertorials, Native ads, Quiz Funnels, Supplements and Top Branded. [L0374.045]
- Tracked brands can be sorted into folders and boards to keep competitor research organized. The account in the example allowed up to 200 brands and displayed the count as used of limit, such as 2 / 200 brands. [L0374.046]
- Brands Tracker lets you follow Meta advertisers, organize them into folders and boards, and track growth, formats, new ads and added dates per brand. The lesson shows a counter of 13 / 200 brands, which suggests a cap on how many brands one account can follow. [L0892.013]
- Winning Hunter Brands lets you follow Meta advertisers, organize them into folders and boards, and open each brand's details. The counter shows how many of the 200 brand slots are in use. [L0772.028]
- The Transcribe ad with AI action sits in the Quick Actions menu of an ad. Longer videos can take about a minute to produce the transcript. Wait until the full script appears before copying it, then check it against the video. [L0374.049]
- The ad details panel and Magic AI offer discovery shortcuts, Find Competitors with Magic AI and Find Shopify Competitors with AI. Use them to move from one winning ad to other advertisers and stores in the same space. Use them as an alternative way to discover competing brands for a product you already want to research. [L0374.051, L0772.041]
- Winning Hunter ad cards have three action buttons. Save ad keeps it in your library. Track in Brand Tracker opens the advertiser's full ad set. Recreate ad with AI generates a new version of the ad. Use Save and Track to build a swipe file of competitor ads before you write your own creatives. [L1164.057]
- Winning Hunter combines Brand Tracker, which shows a brand's personas, USPs, angles and ad copies, with Sales Tracker, which shows a store's revenue estimate, traffic, apps and pixels. Use it to go from an ad found in the Meta Ads Library to a full competitor analysis. [L1164.004]
- Winning Hunter also includes Trends, similar to Exploding Topics, and an AI ad image generator. The lesson presents ad research as the most used part of the platform. [L0892.033]
- Unlimited searches in the TikTok Shop explorer of Winning Hunter are a Premium-plan feature, so heavy product research needs the Premium tier. The explorer also lists trending products, top sellers and new arrivals with the same filters as the main product table. [L0892.035]
- Winning Hunter is listed at $90 per month on the provider's on-screen list but is marked as not included on its sales page. Check the current list and price on the provider's pricing page before budgeting. [L1387.015, L1387.008]

### Kalodata and TikTok research

#### Kalodata

- Kalodata is a TikTok product and ad research tool. It shows products with their revenue, items sold, average unit price, video and ad counts, and category, and lets you open the ad videos to study their hooks. [L0901.005]
- Kalodata shows products with product rank by country, units sold, revenue, average unit price and last-30-day figures, plus linked videos and ads. Use it to check whether a product sells before you invest time in building a store. [L1249.017]
- Kalodata setup: set the region to United States and the date range to Last 30 Days, then open the Video & Ad tab of the product page. The example list showed 4,487 video and ad items for one product. [L0901.004]
- In Kalodata, open the Product Insights panel and set the country dropdown to United States to see the product's rank in United States TikTok. Use the Last 30 Days, Last 90 days and Last 180 days filters to compare current and older sales data. [L0704.020]
- For the example NAD+ berry drink mix, Kalodata showed about 34.92k items sold, an average unit price of $23.19 and a price range of $26.36 to $78.95 for the Last 30 Days window from 07/05/2025 to 08/03/2025. Its Concentration Rate (L30D) was 22.3%, while the NAD+ drops in the other form showed 79%. [L0901.006]
- Kalodata limits how many videos you can download without watermark, based on your plan. When the limit is reached, the tool asks you to buy a recharge plan, so plan which hook and testimonial clips you need before downloading. [L0901.007]
- The Kalodata filter panel has a Save selected filters button and a Submit button, so a filter set such as the book research set can be stored and reused instead of rebuilt each time. [L0354.031]
- The Kalodata product detail page has buttons for Generate Promo Video, View on TikTok, Review Insights and Video Tutorial, plus a 'Why is this product underperforming' prompt. These help with a quick check on a candidate product. [L0354.034]
- Kalodata has a built-in AI assistant that answers research questions about a selected product or category. Typical questions include finding the top 10 competitors at the same price, the main pain points in customer reviews, why a product is not selling well and what could change, a product selection analysis report, a comparison of the product's competitiveness with a similar product, and the recent GMV and sales trends. [L1187.028]
- The Kalodata product detail page is organized into tabs and panels: Overview, Product Card, Reviews, Video Content, Video Toolkit, Creator, SKU Analysis, Concentration, Selling Points and Review Insights. The Product Card shows price range, commission rate, shipping fee, rating and creator count. The Video Toolkit includes Storyboard Export and video generation tools such as Video Remake and Promo Video generation. Shop information and a Local Shop label also appear on the page. [L1187.029]

#### PP Ads

- PP Ads offers a product search section and a large TikTok video library for finding ad clips. It has a trial of 1 USD for 3 days, and users sometimes cancel before the trial converts. [L0363.027]
- PP Ads is a TikTok product and ad research tool used to find competitor listings and ads for a product. It typically yields 5 to 50 or more competitor listings per product, which makes it useful for building a creative folder. [L1377.028]
- PP Ads offers a 3-day trial for $1, after which the subscription bills $155 per month unless cancelled. Cancel the subscription or remove the payment method before the trial ends. [L1377.029]
- Search PP Ads for dropshipper phrasing such as '50% off', 'get yours today' or 'limited time offer' to surface dropshipping ads. Branded ads make up most results in broad searches, so the keyword search finds the right ads faster. [L1377.031]

PP Ads procedure:

1. Open PP Ads and search 5 to 10 generic keywords from the product's name. [L1377.030]
2. Apply the filters: post creation date within the last two weeks, around 500 to 1,000 or more likes, and e-commerce platform Shopify. [L1377.030]
3. Open the listings with the latest ads and download the most recent ad videos for a creative folder. [L1377.030]
4. Aim for at least 5 to 10 competitor creatives, with 7 to 10 as a safe range for testing. [L1377.030]

#### Pixta and TikTok Creative Center

- Pixta is a product research website and app that lists products from TikTok ads, with tabs such as winning ads and product research. Each listing links to a TikTok video or ad, which helps you see whether the product is getting traction. Check the linked content directly on TikTok instead of relying on the listing alone. [L0372.007]
- TikTok Creative Center, reached through TikTok Business Center, shows trending hashtags, trending songs and top-performing ads. Use its filters for industry, country and time window to match your target market. [L0706.016]

### Pinterest, multi-platform and other spy tools

- A Pinterest spy feature inside a product research tool, filtered by weekly winners, shows which products are currently running as Pinterest ads and is used to find test products. [L1135.015]
- PPSpy and Ali Hunter estimate a store's sales and Pinterest activity, such as impressions and saves, to judge whether a competitor is performing well. [L0871.052]
- Minea is a shop-analysis tool that fetches a store's ads from most platforms and shows how many ads run and how a store scales on Facebook. The lesson found 280 ads for one store this way. [L0871.051]
- To analyze a competitor's store in Minea, open Shops, search for the store name and select the matching result to reach Shop analysis. The tool pulls ads from most platforms and shows how many ads the store runs and how it scales, for example 280 ads for one store. [L0871.088]
- Pipiads is most likely the paid product spy tool that shows trending products across platforms. The transcript calls it 'pizza'. It is meant to be added after you have gained some experience with organic content. Its list price is $263 per month. [L0706.020, L1387.003]
- WitTau is a spy tool for YouTube ads that shows which product types are working on the platform. [L0788.039]
- Pizzazz tracks up to five competitor stores at a time: enter the store URL and it shows how many sales the store made in a chosen period. It also has a product spy feature for finding products that are already selling. [L0388.016]

### Store sales and traffic estimators

- PPSpy is a free Chrome extension that shows live sales and lets you track up to five stores for free. It gives product-by-product sales over the last 24 hours and is accurate to roughly 50 to 70 percent. [L0335.012]
- PPSpy is listed at $299 per month on the bundle list. The free tier with five tracked stores and the paid list price apply to different plans, so check which plan includes the features you need. [L0335.012, L1387.003]
- PP Ads is used to estimate how many sales a Shopify store is getting. It only records sales while the app is open and you are browsing that store, so leave it running for a period, up to a couple of hours, before trusting the numbers. [L0372.006]
- Peak Star estimates the revenue of competitor stores by scraping their websites. A reading of about 1,000 sales suggests the store is doing at least that, and 1,500 to 2,000 suggests slightly more. The numbers are rough estimates, not exact figures. [L0639.027]
- In Peakstar, open the third menu item (Dashboard, AI content and store research) to check stores. Enter the product keyword and select the target country, such as United States, to find products that can be tested. The seller was unsure whether the store figures reflect real tracked sales, so verify them before relying on them. [L0678.046]
- Shop Hunter gives product-level sales for a store, which helps identify the individual product driving revenue. Use it together with another tool to confirm numbers. [L0335.051]
- Shop Hunter is an alternative to Peak Star for estimating competitor store sales. Its accuracy compared with other tools was not verified. [L0639.028]
