- Different app, same kind of offer: the Shopify product upsell app shows add-on offers after an item is added to cart and at checkout. It has a 30-day free trial, then costs about 10 to 60 dollars per month, depending on traffic and sales volume [L0923.038].
- Bold Product Options and Bold Product Upsell are the cross-selling and product-option apps recommended for upsell and variant-option needs [L0429.018] (Source A, 2022, older). Both are apps for cross-sell and upsell offers in the Shopify app store [L0963.050] (Source A, 2022, older).
- Bold Product Options and Bold Product Upsells let customers add options and extra items to the order. They are preferred over creating new bundle SKUs in Shopify [L0967.053] (Source A, 2020, older).
- Bold Upsell is used for upselling and Bold Product Options for cross-selling bundles [L1113.033] (Source A, 2022, older).
- Bold Upsell is used for post-purchase upsells. Bold Product Options is used for cross-sells [L0968.046] (Source A, 2021, older).
- Bold Upsell adds upsell and add-on products, and is used to increase average order value [L1087.003] (Source A, 2020, older). The speaker reported long-term use without issues [L1088.067] (Source A, 2020, older).
- Bold Cross Sell shows add-on products as add-to-cart options on the product page, which raises cart value. Use it for add-ons the buyer needs that are too expensive to give away. Example: a 200 USD product with 30 USD straps and a 50 USD cover raises the cart to 280 USD [L1110.027] (Source A, 2019, older).
- Upsells and cross-sells on the product and post-purchase pages were built with custom code on Shopify, with Bold Upsell for upsell functions [L1109.039] (Source A, 2018, older).
- Rule: enable the cross-sell app only after you have selected a collection for it, otherwise it has nothing to show. It is not needed on a demo store and is covered once the store has live products [L0976.013].

#### Bold Sales Motivator (threshold display)
- Bold Sales Motivator shows a shopper how close they are to a free bonus, for example in a spend X to get X offer, so the threshold is visible and encourages the shopper to reach it [L0757.030].
- It shows how far shoppers are from a cart threshold as they add items. Use it for offers such as a free gift over 1,000 dollars, and turn it off as soon as the promotion ends [L1204.050].

### Cart drawers and cart-stage upsells

#### Cart drawers
- Slide Cart (Slide Cart Drawer) slides the cart out over the current page. Its cart function is free, while its upsell function is paid [L0339.005].
- The cart drawer opens on the same page as the product, so customers do not need to go to a separate cart page and press back. Cart and checkout feel faster and more fluid [L0339.006].
- Monster Cart Upsell is a cart app that shows complementary product offers and slide-out cart blocks. It works best when the store has several complementary products [L0871.048].
- Upcart automates upsell prompts at checkout when the cart value reaches set thresholds, encouraging customers to add items or upgrade to higher bundles, which increases AOV [L0232.007].
- After a customer clicks add to cart, a product recommendation app can show related items on the cart step to raise average order value. Use it alongside a post-purchase upsell page, not instead of one. The app named in the lesson for the post-purchase upsell is AfterSell [L1131.060].

#### Free-gift threshold messages
- Shopify apps can show a cart message that says how much more is needed to unlock a free gift. Search the Shopify App Store for free gift threshold apps [L1094.060] (Source A, 2023).
- Monster App adds free gift offers to orders, but its usage fees are high. Operators may look for a theme with built-in free gift options to replace it [L1173.015].

#### Kaching Cart
- Kaching Cart is a Shopify app that controls the cart and checkout entry point. It adds urgency messages, timers, promotion progress bars, upsells and trust badges [L0560.010].
- Cart settings sections in the left menu: General, Top bar, Timer, Promotions progress bar, A/B test, Edit translations, Product upsell, Products in cart, Side product upsell, Footer, Discount, Sticky Cart and Kaching Upsell [L0560.011].
- Make sure Cart is enabled is on. Cart mode is set to Previewing items in this setup, and Cart is enabled must be switched on before any other cart setting takes effect [L0560.011, L0560.036].
- Side product upsell: on mobile, the side upsell appears inside the cart drawer and can be dragged into position. Options include Hide upsell if product already in cart, Automatically remove when the cart is empty, Automatically add the upsell product and Show Saved N badge [L0560.025].
- Upsell toggles (used for shipping protection): set Display mode to Standard in checkout button. Fill Text when is not in the cart with ((upsell_toggle_price)) ((upsell_toggle_title)) and Text when is in the cart with Includes ((upsell_toggle_price)) ((upsell_toggle_title)). The add-on then appears as a toggle next to the checkout button [L0560.035].

#### Kaching Cart setup sequence
1. Open Apps > Kaching Cart > Cart settings and confirm Cart is enabled.
2. Under Top bar, enter a warning message about stock scarcity.
3. Under Timer, set the countdown to 3 minutes and turn on Reset timer when products are added to cart.
4. Under Promotions progress bar, set the threshold, the reward and any tiers.
5. Under Trust badges, upload the Trustpilot badge image and set it to show under the checkout button.
6. Switch off Shipping protection and Footer while testing a product.
7. Go to Settings > Checkout > Editor > Header > Logo and upload the Trustpilot-formatted logo.
8. Go to Online Store > Themes > Edit default theme content, search for checkout, find Checkout alternative payment method banner and enter a low-stock message. [L0560.012]

### Rebuy Smart Cart

#### What it is
- Rebuy Smart Cart is an app-based, conversion-focused cart that replaces the default cart of a Shopify theme, either a full-page cart or a slide-out cart. It adds features the theme may lack, such as personalized recommendations, progress bars, upsells and subscription options [L1231.001].
- The recommendations block is powered by Rebuy data sources and its AI recommendation engine. The merchant defines the rules that decide which products appear. The cart also contains a tiered progress bar and an announcement bar, and recommendations can be adjusted in the Rebuy admin [L1231.002].
- The in-cart cross-sell widget is created automatically with each new smart cart and recommends products in the cart. Recommendations are powered by Rebuy's AI technology by default [L1230.018]. A new smart cart automatically gets a cross-sell widget [L1230.002].

#### Versions
- Rebuy no longer lets merchants create new v1 Smart Carts. Existing v1 carts are marked with a Legacy label. A v1 cart can still be duplicated and the duplicate can be edited, but a new cart built from scratch will be v2 [L1231.009].
- The new cart starts as v2 [L1231.008].

#### Creating a Smart Cart (v2)
1. Open Smart Cart in the left navigation under Merchandising Widgets.
2. Click New Smart Cart; the new cart starts as v2.
3. Choose either Single Column or Double Column as the layout.
4. Click Create.
5. Configure the cart from top to bottom.
6. Use the preview to check the mobile and desktop views.
7. Toggle Live only when the cart is ready. [L1231.008]

- Older procedure, same flow with more detail: 1) In the Rebuy admin, open Smart Cart in the left menu and click New Smart Cart. 2) Give the smart cart a name. 3) Choose Single Column or Double Column layout and click Create. 4) Work through the editor's five sections: Fixed Top Bar, Header, Goal Box, Body, Footer. 5) In the Fixed Top Bar, adjust the cart title and the login button. 6) In the Header, click Add and select Announcement Bar. 7) In the Goal Box, click Add and select Tiered Progress Bar. 8) In the Body, click into the Cart Items block for its advanced options. 9) In the Footer, click Add and select Discount Code Input. 10) Drag any draggable component to reposition it. 11) Confirm all changes are saved using the top-right control. 12) Switch the toggle to make the smart cart live. 13) Test the cart on the storefront. [L1230.001]
- Layout choice is Single Column or Double Column. The cart is named before it is created [L1230.002].
- The Smart Cart list shows all drafts with Preview, Live toggle and Duplicate options. Use Duplicate to copy an existing cart instead of building a new one [L1231.010].

#### Cart anatomy (v2)

| Section | Components |
|---|---|
| Fixed top bar | Cart title, login button, mobile/desktop preview, Live toggle |
| Header | Announcement bar, tiered progress bar, discount code input, order notes, custom code block, cross-sell widget (components added via Add Component to Header) |
| Goal box | Area between header and body where the tiered progress bar is typically placed |
| Body | Cart items, empty cart language, subscription upgrade options, cross-sell widgets |
| Footer | Cart subtotal with singular and plural labels |
| Checkout area | Terms and conditions, pre-purchase pop-up, view cart button, continue shopping button, Shop Pay, accelerated checkout buttons, installment payment links |
| Global settings | Custom CSS input, event callbacks (JavaScript event listeners) |
[L1231.020]

#### Editing components
- Fixed Top Bar > Cart Title: rename the default title to fit the store's wording or theme, e.g. a brand-specific cart title instead of a generic one [L1230.003].
- Fixed Top Bar > Login Button: enabled by default so customers can log in from the cart. To remove it, use the Hide option on the button [L1230.004]. The login button can be shown to logged-out customers, or used with customer rules based on login status or account tags for segment-specific promotions [L1231.018].
- Add components via Add Component to Header. Options: Announcement Bar, Cross-Sell Widget, Custom Code Block, Discount Code Input, Order Notes, Tiered Progress Bar and Smart Cart Apps from partner integrations [L1231.021].
- Smart Cart > Goal Box > Add Component to Goal Box lists Announcement Bar, Cross-Sell Widget, Custom Code Block, Discount Code Input, Order Notes and Tiered Progress Bar. When no Smart Cart apps are installed, the same panel shows an Add Smart Cart App button instead [L1230.027].
- Discount Code Input: add it in the Footer via Add and Discount Code Input [L1230.001] (see procedure above).
- Body > Cart Items > Empty Cart Language: sets the heading and text shown when the cart is empty. A Shop Now link can send customers to a chosen page so they can start shopping immediately [L1230.013].
- Cart Items > Use Product Metafields: a developer option that lets the cart pull custom product data. It is only useful if custom metafield data is already in use [L1230.016].
- Switch to Subscription, Product Metafields and Buy More Save More need additional configuration in the editor before they can be published. Keep them disabled until that setup is complete, and follow their setup guides before enabling them [L1230.017].
- Every component has a Custom Template toggle. When on, the template code is visible and editable inside Rebuy, so customizations are stored in Rebuy rather than the Shopify theme, which makes support easier [L1231.016].
- Every component has a hide icon that hides it without deleting it, so it can be shown again later. This works like the Shopify theme customizer [L1231.017].
- Cart subtotal wording: click Cart Subtotal in the footer, open LANGUAGE OPTIONS, and edit Subtotal Label (singular) and Subtotal Label (plural). The item count can be inserted as a variable. Keep the other settings under ADVANCED unchanged unless you need them [L1231.065].
- Global settings: custom CSS input for styling the cart, and event callbacks for JavaScript event listeners to run custom behavior [L1231.055].
- Partner integrations extend the cart with social proof and other functions. Examples are reviews apps such as Loox, Yotpo and Judge.me. The partner list includes Klaviyo and Route for better performance [L1231.056].

#### Checkout area and footer
- Smart Cart footer > Checkout Area Options: under Pre-Checkout Options you can turn on Terms & Conditions and Pre-Purchase Pop-Up. Standard Buttons holds the Checkout Button. Labels and Routing holds the View Cart Button and Continue Shopping Button. Express Checkout Buttons holds Shop Pay and Accelerated Checkout. Additional Options holds Installments [L1230.025].

#### Tiered progress bar and free products
- Keep Use Shopify Functions (Recommended) switched on. It is on by default under Advanced Bar Settings, powers both product tiers and discount tiers, and is the basis for turning full-priced products into free gifts. The gift area has its own title field under Free Gifts [L1230.011, L1308.001]. Keep it on unless you have a specific reason to switch it off [L1308.001].
- The Use Shopify Functions toggle on a Free Product tier creates a Shopify Functions based discount automatically. This removes the need for a Shopify automatic discount with custom logic or a Shopify Script [L1231.032].
- Shopify Scripts are being phased out, so discounts that need custom logic should move to Shopify Functions. Rebuy's Functions-based discounts can replace an extra third-party discount app for free product and discount tiers [L1231.058].
- Mistake: a free shipping tier in the progress bar does not make shipping free by itself. Shipping rates must also be changed in Shopify shipping settings, or free shipping will not apply at checkout [L1230.010].
- The free shipping tier is a visual element only, with no link to Shopify Functions, so it creates no discount. To give a real free reward, change the tier to a free product tier [L1308.002].
- Mistake: a product tier with no product selected causes the Tiered Progress Bar to be omitted from the Smart Cart, and an error message appears. Select a product for every product tier, or hide the component [L1308.004].
- Path: Smart Cart V2 (Main) > Tiered Progress Bar > Configure Tiers > Tier 1. Under Tier 1, use Search Products to pick the product, set the Unlock Price (example: $50), and use Add New Tier to create more tiers. Save the Smart Cart after each change [L1308.005].
- Free-product reward procedure:
1) Open the Smart Cart, go to the gold box section and add a Tiered Progress Bar.
2) Keep Use Shopify Functions (Recommended) switched on.
3) Switch the free shipping tier to a free product tier.
4) Search for an active, in-stock product that is available on the online store, select it and set the Unlock Price.
5) Save, then preview the Smart Cart and add items until the unlock price is reached.
6) Open the checkout and confirm the reward product is fully discounted. [L1308.006]
- Product Discount Text: path Tiered Progress Bar > Configure Labels & Text > Product Discount Text. Enter the text that should appear at checkout for the free product reward. If empty, Shopify shows the default name product discount [L1308.007].
- Mistake: on checkout, the product discount text is taken only from the first product tier, even when several product tiers exist. Give the first tier a generic text such as free gift, because a specific name such as a sandwich will be wrong for the other tiers. Check the tooltip in the tier settings to see this behavior [L1308.012].
- Emojis can be typed into any text field, including the discount text. On Mac press Control+Command+Space, on Windows press the Windows key and period, then search for the emoji (for example a sandwich emoji) [L1308.008].
- Discount text update procedure:
1) Save the changed discount text in the Smart Cart.
2) Refresh the checkout page, because the new text only shows after a reload.
3) Check that the text appears next to the discounted item. [L1308.009]
- Label wording under Configure Labels & Text: the Amount Remaining Label carries a sentence such as "you are ((remaining_amount)) short of ((discount_amount)) off the whole order". The Amount Reached Label is shown once the goal is met, e.g. "you have earned ((discount_amount)) off the full order". Product-tier entries insert ((product_title)), while discount-tier entries insert ((discount_amount)) [L1308.018].
- Toggles Use Custom Tier Label, Use Amount Remaining Label and Use Amount Reached Label control whether custom texts appear. Enable them to replace the default messages [L1308.019].

#### Preview and go-live
- Save all changes before switching the smart cart live, since the live toggle publishes the current configuration. The toggle is in the top-right area of the editor [L1230.023].
- Simulate Products in Cart adds test products to the editor preview, so components that only appear with items in the cart, such as the footer and subtotal, can be seen while editing. It also shows how the progress bar reacts without placing real orders [L1230.022, L1308.020].
- The Preview button opens the live online store showing the draft cart, with a bar across the bottom indicating Rebuy preview mode. The visual editor may show fonts and global CSS differently than the store, so check the final result on the store [L1231.013].
- Mistake: the Smart Cart editor preview does not show how Functions discounts change the cart total. Test discounts on the checkout page or in the storefront preview of the cart instead [L1308.021].
- To preview a specific Smart Cart on the storefront, use the store URL with the parameters preview_smart_cart=<smart cart ID>, viewcart=true and cart_version=2. This shows that cart's discounts even when it is not the live cart [L1308.022].
- Troubleshooting: if the smart cart does not appear, the theme's native cart drawer still shows, or both the theme's cart and the smart cart show at once, the cart is not set up correctly. Rebuy provides a separate troubleshooting walkthrough for these cases [L1230.024].
- Mobile and desktop views should both be checked before going live [L1231.008].

### Post-purchase offers

#### Tools
- OCU by Zipify is a post-purchase funnel builder considered the best-in-class option, with premium pricing [L0291.011]. One Click Upsell is an alternative with similar functions that may cost more [L0693.027].
- One Click Upsell is a Shopify app that shows extra offers right after checkout, on the thank-you and order-status pages. Customers accept with one click, which raises average order value (AOV) per customer [L1141.001].
- OCU is a post-purchase upsell app that shows extra offers after checkout. It can sell more of the same product or add other products to the order [L1107.033].
- AfterSell is a budget-friendly post-purchase funnel platform with all essential features for building upsell and downsell sequences [L0291.012].
- Reconvert is a post-purchase funnel builder for constructing upsell funnels [L0291.013]. It is also a post-purchase upsell app used to offer extra products after checkout [L0693.027].
- ReConvert is a post-purchase app for upsells and a post-purchase survey. The survey asks buyers why they bought and which use cases matter to them. It is repeated weekly to reveal the strongest marketing angle [L1218.021].
- KickFunnels is a one-time offer upsell funnel builder with built-in downsell features, used for product funnels with multiple upsells and conversion-optimized pages. Shopify alternative: Zipify Pages OCU (one-click upsell app) [L0307.020].
- Zipify is a post-purchase funnel tool that can offer a discounted repeat purchase right after checkout [L0363.028]. Zipify and Convert are apps used to build post-purchase upsell and cross-sell funnels after checkout [L0871.049].

#### OCU funnel setup
1. Install One Click Upsell from the Shopify app store and open its dashboard.
2. Go to Upsell Funnels and click Add New Funnel, or create a Catch-All Funnel in one click.
3. Choose what is sold after checkout: an ebook or other digital product, a similar product, or the same product the customer just bought.
4. Set the triggers that open the funnel (see the trigger settings).
5. Add Post-Purchase Upsell Offer 1 and set its discount, usually 30-40% off the added item.
6. Add Upsell 2 (recommended product) and the Post-Purchase Downsell as the final step.
7. Edit each offer: heading, countdown timer, product, shipping and buttons.
8. Check the preview and run the price check before going live.
9. Enable the funnel and track its views, conversion and revenue. [L1141.002]

#### Offer editor
- Blocks in the Post-Purchase Upsell Editor: Display Offer, Heading, Countdown Timer, Upsell Product and Add Content Block. Fields include Background Color, Full width, Image Size, Image Resizing, Fit, Image Border, Badge Label, Badge Color, Badge Position (Above Product Title), Discount (Percentage), Savings Label, Hide Quantity Selector, Predefined Quantity, Buttons Position (set to Accept above decline) and Payment Disclaimer [L1141.015].
- Countdown Timer block: set Time Limit to 3 minutes. This gives urgency without feeling rushed [L1141.016].
- Price block: the original price is struck through, followed by the discounted price and a savings label such as 25% Savings (Discount set to Percentage, with a Savings Label field). The accept button reads Pay now followed by the discounted total. The countdown label reads Exclusive offer expires in: above the timer [L1141.034].
- Post-purchase offers inherit the Shopify checkout styles automatically, so set checkout branding in Shopify before judging how an offer looks. The offer editor preview uses a product placeholder until a real product is selected in the upsell product block. Preview settings can be adjusted to match the checkout more closely [L1141.035].

#### Digital upsell as Funnel 2
1. Create the PDF or ebook as its own product in Shopify.
2. In the upsell funnel, click Add Product(s) and select that product.
3. Add a further upsell with a compatible product that complements what the customer is buying. [L1141.020]

#### Follow-up across channels
1. Show the one-time upsell offer on the post-purchase page immediately after checkout.
2. Send an SMS through an SMS app such as SMS Bump at the same time, reminding the buyer that the order is being prepared and that the upsell is available for a limited time.
3. Send an email through Klaviyo at the same time with the same offer and a countdown.
4. Retarget the buyers with Facebook ads for the upsell offer. [L0789.032]
- SMS and email text should state a deadline, such as 20 minutes left to buy the upsell at 14 dollars instead of 49 dollars, and the remaining stock count, such as only five left [L0789.033].
- Post-purchase discount ranges differ by example: 30 to 40% off the added item in the OCU setup [L1141.002], and a 14 dollar price against a 49 dollar original in the follow-up example [L0789.033].

### Checkout replacement

- Better Cart is a third-party checkout app that replaces the Shopify checkout. It is worth considering when monthly sales exceed about 20,000 USD. Its monthly fee then costs less than Shopify checkout fees, and it allows upsells and bump offers that Shopify's checkout does not [L0387.004].
- Switching from Shopify's checkout to a replacement checkout app is typically cheaper once monthly revenue is above roughly 20,000 USD, because you pay the app's monthly fee instead of Shopify checkout fees [L0387.005].
- Below that revenue level, keep Shopify checkout and use the cart and post-purchase tools above, since upsells and bump offers can be added at the cart and after purchase [L0387.004, L0789.032].

### Retention and subscription platforms

- ReCharge is an all-in-one app platform that combines subscriptions, product bundles, rewards programs and customer analytics in one place. It is used to raise average order value, reduce churn and increase lifetime value [L1232.001].
- Procedure:
1. Run all retention programs (subscriptions, bundles, rewards) inside one platform.
2. Test quickly to see which program moves the key metric.
3. Keep the programs that work for the specific store and drop the rest.
4. Use the unified customer data and analytics view to optimize offers continuously. [L1232.006]

### Choosing an upsell or bundle app

- Choose by budget first. AfterSell suits a budget under $20 per month, ReConvert comes next, and Zipify OCU is the choice for advanced features. Post-purchase upsell apps such as ReConvert, AfterSell and Zipify OCU offer similar plans with 30-day free trials. [L1411.020]
- Most of these upsell and bundle apps start with a 14-day or 30-day free trial, then charge a monthly fee. Compare the monthly price against your budget before installing, because the cheapest option can be under $10 per month and the advanced option can cost $35 per month. [L1411.029]
- Install a bundle app when you sell bundles, since bundles are a core part of the store's average order value approach. [L1327.031]
- Kaching's post-purchase upsell is an advanced tool. Install it when scaling, not at the foundation stage. [L1327.033]
- Some sellers prefer single-purpose apps. Vitals bundles many apps, including upsell tools, in one package, and it is a good choice if it covers all the upsell and cross-sell features you need. [L1410.010, L1412.025]
- Vitals is an app suite that bundles upsells and cross-sells into one subscription. It suits a store that wants many apps together, but some sellers prefer a single-purpose app. [L1412.025]
- Some post-purchase upsell app support teams will build a funnel for you if you send them your requirements. This reduces setup time for non-technical sellers. [L1410.049]

### Bundle apps

#### Comparison

| App | What it does | Trial and price | Source ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bundle Bear | Volume-discount bundles, with revenue shown per offer | 14-day trial; plans from $24.99 per month | L1345.020, L1411.011, L1411.012 |
| Wide Bundle / Wide Bundles | Bundle layout, labels and discount display | 14-day trial, then $16 per month (Wide Bundles) | L1345.026, L1411.010 |
| Catching Bundle (called Coaching Bundles in other lessons) | Simple bundle app, described as the cheapest of the three | 14-day trial; $9 per month billed yearly or $14.99 per month billed monthly | L1411.013, L1410.006, L1412.012 |
| White Bundles | Labels such as free shipping and custom savings text | Not stated | L1410.006, L1412.011 |
| Kaching Bundles | Product bundles with quantity breaks and a highlighted best-selling bundle | Not stated | L1324.008, L1327.031 |
| Frequently Bought Together (older advice, 2021-2022) | Bundle box on the product page | Not stated | L1409.008, L1409.031 |

- The sources spell the Catching Bundle app two ways, "Catching Bundle" and "Coaching Bundles". Confirm the exact name in the Shopify App Store before you search. [L1411.013, L1410.006, L1412.012]

#### Bundle Bear

- Bundle Bear is a Shopify app for volume-discount bundles, such as buy-two-get-a-percentage-off deals (for example 40% off). It is one of the common apps for this offer type. [L1345.020]
- Bundle Bear creates product bundles with volume discounts and editable offer text. You can change the button text, for example from "Add to cart" to "Get my deal". The lesson reports high conversion with it and calls it simple to use. [L1412.010]
- Bundle Bear shows revenue per offer, so you can see which offer performs. [L1411.011]
- Example tiers from Bundle Bear are buy 2 get 10% off and buy 3 get 15% off. [L1411.012]
- Bundle Bear can combine products on the same product page or with items from another collection, for example a ring with a matching necklace. One store reported a clear rise in average order value after adding it. [L1428.022]
- Bundle Bear's drawback is that it creates a discount for every order that already has a discount, which one seller found annoying. [L1428.022]
- **Installation conflict:** The sources disagree. Two lessons say Bundle Bear needs a code line pasted into the Shopify product page template. [L1410.006, L1412.010] A third says it needs no added code, installs on any theme and works with page builders such as PageFly. [L1411.012] Use the code-line route if you edit the product template directly. Use the no-code route if you build the page with a page builder. [L1410.006, L1412.010, L1411.012]

#### Procedure: first volume-discount offer

1. Install a bundle or volume-discount app from the Shopify App Store. No code is needed, and it works on most themes and page builders. [L1411.028]
2. Create a volume-discount offer, for example buy 2 get 10% off and buy 3 get 15% off. [L1411.028]
3. Customize the label and layout of the offer. [L1411.028]
4. Place the offer on the product page or in the cart drawer. [L1411.028]
5. Check revenue per offer in the app's analytics and compare offers before keeping one. [L1411.028]

#### Procedure: compare bundle apps and layouts

1. Search the Shopify App Store for "bundle" or "bundle offer". [L1345.091]
2. Sort or pick the apps with the most reviews. [L1345.091]
3. Open each app and check its demo layout. [L1345.091]
4. Compare the layout with the competitor's bundle layout to find the closest match. [L1345.091]

#### Wide Bundle and Wide Bundles

- Wide Bundle is a Shopify bundle app that shows a bundle layout and volume discount. Its layout can be compared with a competitor's bundle layout to find an inspiration template. [L1345.026]
- Wide Bundles is a Shopify app for bundle and volume-discount offers, with a customizable layout, labels and discount display. It has a 14-day free trial and costs $16 per month after that. [L1411.010]

#### White Bundles and the Catching or Coaching Bundle app

- White Bundles adds labels such as free shipping and savings text such as "you save $35". [L1410.006]
- White Bundles offers more options than the simpler alternatives, such as custom text like "you save X dollars". [L1412.011]
- The Coaching Bundles app is cheaper and simpler than Bundle Bear, and it needs no code pasted into the page template. [L1410.006, L1412.012]
- Catching Bundle has a 14-day free trial and costs $9 per month billed yearly or $14.99 per month billed monthly. It was described as the cheapest of the three bundle apps. [L1411.013]

#### Kaching Bundles

- Kaching Bundles is a Shopify app for creating product bundles with quantity breaks. It highlights a best-selling bundle option on the product page. [L1324.008]
- Install Kaching Bundles when you sell bundles, since bundles are a core part of the store's average order value approach. [L1327.031]

#### Frequently Bought Together (older advice, 2021-2022)

- Frequently Bought Together is a Shopify app that adds a bundle box to the product page. The box shows the core product with related products and a bundled price. It can be placed anywhere on the page, usually below the Add to cart button. [L1409.008]
- In the box, the core product is labelled "THIS ITEM" and each related product has a selectable checkbox. The customer ticks the add-ons and presses "Add Selected to Cart". The box shows a bundled price for the selected items, and each product can show a sale price beside its regular price. [L1409.031]
- Other on-page bundle apps exist and can be used if they work well. Any on-page app should let each core product have its own specific upsells, matching the upsell planner sheet. [L1409.011]

### Product-page cross-sells (older advice, 2021-2022)

- Use a "customers who bought this also bought this" style recommendation widget on product pages to drive upsells and multi-product purchases. Shopify apps exist that provide this feature. [L1522.016]
- Shopify apps can provide both recommendation widgets and product bundles on product pages and on standalone bundle pages. Use them to set up cross-sells and value bundles without custom development. [L1522.018]

### Free gifts and cart progress

#### Free gift apps

- Easy Gift is a Shopify app that adds a free gift automatically when a product is added to the cart. It shows a pop-up and size selection. [L1315.033]
- Monster Cart is a cart app that can add free gifts and bundles. It opens in the cart and offers the free gift for adding to the order. [L1315.033, L1363.020]
- Free gift apps are optional. The lesson's alternative is to show the free gift in an image on the landing page, with the text "your order comes with a free gift, automatically added to cart" and the gift's title. [L1363.020]
- Free gift apps are not interchangeable. Use the one that fits your store and check its behaviour on the product and cart pages. [L1315.036]
- Two cart apps may conflict. A pop-up from one free gift app may not work with another cart app's own cart. Test the combination before running traffic. [L1315.035]

#### Checking the savings display

- Some gift apps show savings from the full compare-at price instead of the price you actually charge. This overstates the saving and makes the value look inflated. Check that the displayed saving matches the price charged. [L1315.034]
- Monster Cart shows savings based on the compare-at price, which some gift apps do not do. [L1315.033]

#### Cart progress and free shipping

- Monster Cart Upsell offers free-shipping thresholds, free gifts, percent-off tiers, cross-sells, bundles and pre-selected extras. [L1412.021]
- Monster Cart Upsell includes free-shipping upsells as one of its cart offers. [L1410.007]
- Monster Cart Upsell can add shipping offers alongside cross-sell items. [L1411.015]

### Cart drawers and cart upsells

#### Monster Cart Upsell

- Monster Cart Upsell is a cart drawer app for cart upsells, free-shipping upsells, tiered offers, pre-selected add-ons and bundles on the cart page. It has a 10-day free trial and chat support. It is somewhat complex and suits simple funnels. [L1410.007]
- Monster Cart Upsell is a Shopify app for the cart drawer. It adds cross-sell items, several products at once, and shipping offers, and it allows detailed customization of the cart messages. [L1411.015]
- Monster Cart Upsell supports multiple conditions. It is more complex than simple bundle apps, but its chat support can help build funnels. [L1412.021]

### Post-purchase upsell apps

#### Zipify One-Click Upsell (OCU)

- *Older advice, 2021-2022:* One-Click Upsell (Zipify OCU) handles pre-purchase and post-purchase upsells, including cart-page upsells. It lets you design the upsell page, set specific upsells per product, and split test offers and quantities. [L1409.012]
- Zipify OCU is built by the company Zipify, and Shopify promotes it as part of its own platform. It still has to be installed from the Shopify App Store. [L1409.030]
- **Price conflict:** The 2021-2022 source says One-Click Upsell is a paid app from $24.99 per month with a 30-day free trial. Additional charges may apply, and the cost is considered reasonable once the app handles meaningful sales volume. [L1409.013] The Source C lesson says Zipify OCU has a 30-day free trial and then costs $35 per month, and it is the more advanced option with features such as custom sections. [L1411.017] The lessons do not say which plan each figure belongs to, so check the current App Store listing before budgeting. [L1409.013, L1411.017]
- Zipify OCU is somewhat more expensive than ReConvert and AfterSale, and it is an alternative to both. [L1412.024]
- Other post-purchase upsell apps exist in the Shopify App Store. Search for "post-purchase upsell" and compare them with One-Click Upsell before choosing. [L1409.014]

#### ReConvert Upsell

- ReConvert Upsell is a post-purchase upsell app with 24/7 chat support. Its funnel can be duplicated for other offers. [L1410.008]
- ReConvert offers post-purchase surveys to learn why a customer bought and their use case, which is useful for Facebook interest targeting. It also offers birthday gift options. [L1410.008]
- ReConvert Upsell and Cross Sell builds post-purchase funnels in which each offer leads to a different offer, depending on whether the customer accepts. It supports page and section customization, tutorials and customer support. [L1411.018]
- Reconvert lets you duplicate a finished funnel and reuse it for other offers. It also supports post-purchase surveys, a birthday gift option, thank-you page customization and 24/7 support. [L1412.022]

#### AfterSell

- AfterSell is a cheaper post-purchase upsell app at about $7.99 per month with a 30-day free trial. Each funnel must be built from scratch, unlike ReConvert's duplication feature. [L1410.009, L1412.023]
- AfterSell is a budget option, suggested for a budget under $20 per month. Its features are similar to ReConvert and Zipify OCU. [L1411.019]
- The sources spell the app AfterSell, AfterSale and After Sale. They refer to the same app. [L1410.009, L1411.019, L1412.023]

#### Post-purchase comparison

| App | Price and trial | Main points | Source ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| AfterSell | About $7.99 per month, 30-day trial; suggested budget under $20 per month | Funnels built from scratch | L1410.009, L1412.023, L1411.019 |
| ReConvert | Price not stated in the sources | Duplicable funnels, surveys, birthday gifts, 24/7 support | L1410.008, L1412.022, L1411.018 |
| Zipify OCU | 30-day trial; $35 per month (Source C). From $24.99 per month with 30-day trial (2021-2022 source) | Advanced features, custom sections, cart-page upsells | L1411.017, L1409.013, L1409.012 |
| Kaching post-purchase upsell | Not stated | Advanced tool, installed when scaling | L1327.033 |

### Rebuy: Smart Cart and post-purchase flows

#### Clean up old Smart Cart discounts

1. Set a Smart Cart without product or discount tiers as the live cart. [L1308.023]
2. Confirm that the discounts no longer apply, because Functions follows the settings of the live cart. [L1308.023]
3. Open Shopify Admin > Discounts and delete the old Rebuy discounts there. Clicking one redirects to the Rebuy admin. [L1308.023]

- Deleting or switching a Smart Cart does not remove the old Functions discounts from Shopify Admin automatically. Leftover entries stay in the Discounts list but do not apply when the active cart has no tiers. Delete them manually to keep the list clean. [L1308.024]

#### Test a Smart Cart copy without affecting live customers

1. Duplicate the live Smart Cart in the Rebuy admin. The test copy has the same layout but no effect on live customers. [L1308.027]
2. Change the discount tiers in the copy, for example to 4% order discount tiers, and save it. [L1308.027]
3. Open the store with `preview_smart_cart=<Smart Cart ID>`, `viewcart=true` and `cart_version=2` in the URL. This shows that copy's discounts at checkout, without switching the live cart. [L1308.027]

#### Post-purchase flows: how they work

- In Rebuy, upsells are offers shown right after a customer accepts an earlier offer. Downsells are offers shown after the customer declines one. Both live inside a post-purchase flow, and the flow pulls in widgets that hold the actual offers. [L1413.001]
- In the Rebuy admin, open the Post Purchase section in the left navigation. It has two areas. Post Purchase Flows is where the complete upsell and downsell sequences are built. Post Purchase Widgets is where the individual offers are created and then referenced inside flows. [L1413.003]
- The Rebuy admin left menu lists Dashboard, Widgets, Products, Landing Pages, Data Sources, Reports and Integrations, plus a Post Purchase section. Under Post Purchase, the three areas are Post Purchase Flows, Post Purchase Widgets and Post Purchase Analytics. Help Docs sits under Support. [L1413.017]
- Each flow in the Post Purchase Flows list shows a status of LIVE or NOT LIVE. Only the flow marked LIVE is shown to customers, so test and draft flows stay NOT LIVE until you switch them on. [L1413.018]

#### Procedure: build a post-purchase flow

1. Open the Rebuy admin and go to Post Purchase > Post Purchase Flows. [L1413.004]
2. Open an existing flow or create a new one. [L1413.004]
3. Set the initial offer, the first widget shown to every customer who reaches the checkout confirmation. [L1413.004]
4. Add a downsell branch that appears when the initial offer is declined. [L1413.004]
5. Add an upsell branch that appears when the initial offer is accepted. [L1413.004]
6. Chain further upsells and downsells off any accepted or declined offer to create multi-layer flows. [L1413.004]

#### When offers appear

- The initial offer in a post-purchase flow is shown in the Shopify checkout as soon as the customer clicks the Pay Now button. Widget settings and data sources decide which exact offer each customer sees. [L1413.005]
- Rebuy flows show offers only on the checkout confirmation page. They add revenue without changing the customer's original order. Keep upsell and downsell offers separate in the flow so the customer always sees the next logical step. [L1413.016]

#### Example: BOGO initial offer and downsell

- A simple initial offer can be a buy-one-get-one deal with 25% off the second product. Its rule can trigger when the cart contains the input product, and it is set up as the first widget in the flow. [L1413.006]
- Downsell setup: after a customer declines the 25% BOGO initial offer, show a second BOGO offer at 50% off with the message "Last chance, grab it 50% off". This second offer is a downsell because it appears only after a decline. [L1413.007]

## Product Feeds, Google Merchant Center and Shopping Feed Apps

### Before Connecting Anything: Store Readiness and Policy Checks

- Google Gemini can pre-check the store and its policy pages before submission to Google Merchant Center and Google Ads. It is aligned with Google's own requirements and can flag placeholders, missing information and unclear policy wording that Google's crawlers would catch. [L0167.017]
- Use Gemini, not ChatGPT, to check a Shopify store against Google Ads and Merchant Center policies. Give it the store's home page URL, ask it to check every policy page, and ask whether the store is fully compliant. [L0808.026]
- An AI assistant such as Gemini can compare the website's shipping and return policy text against the answers entered in Merchant Center to check compliance before saving. Use it as a cross-check, then verify the match yourself. [L0729.017]

**Policy-by-policy check with Gemini:**

1. Start with the Returns/Refunds policy and paste the full text into Gemini. [L0167.018]
2. Apply Gemini's recommended changes in Shopify. [L0167.018]
3. Repeat the same process for the Shipping, Privacy, Terms and Contact pages. [L0167.018]
4. After all updates, ask Gemini to re-check and confirm that the pages are clean. [L0167.018]

**Prompt for the policy review:**

```
Hi, I run YOURDOMAIN.com and am getting ready to send the website to Google Merchant Center and Google Ads for review. Please go through the site and the policy pages I share and point out anything that breaks the rules or looks doubtful. For each issue, state what it is and what update you recommend. Pay particular attention to stand-in text, text in square brackets used as filler, made-up or placeholder contact information (such as dummy phone numbers), vague return or delivery terms, missing or unclear shipping charge details, and absent quick-glance summary information. Where you can, supply the exact replacement wording. Once I have made the changes, I'll paste the revised text back to you, so please tell me whether the page is then fully compliant.
```
[L0167.019]

**Compliance checklist, in this order, before setting up any Google accounts:**

1. Remove every sample product under Shopify Admin > Products. [L0167.021]
2. Click through every header, homepage, collection and footer link and repair any 404 errors. [L0167.021]
3. Take out placeholder pictures and incomplete sections so the storefront appears finished. [L0167.021]
4. Go through all Shopify policy pages under Settings > Policies and strip out any placeholder text. [L0167.021]
5. Put real email, phone and opening hours into the contact details on the policy pages. [L0167.021]
6. Pass each policy page through Google Gemini and implement every fix it recommends. [L0167.021]
7. Revise the Contact Us page so it shows phone, email and hours in addition to the form. [L0167.021]
8. Recheck with Gemini until it reports everything as compliant, and only then create the Google accounts. [L0167.021]

### Choosing a Feed App

**Principles**

- Product feeds to Google Merchant Center should be sent with a separate dedicated app, because it gives more control than the Google & YouTube app's Merchant Center section. [L0722.010]
- A paid feed app gives more control over product data than a free one. Better product data generally leads to better results in Google Shopping, so the monthly fee is usually worth it as sales grow. [L1165.006]
- Feed management apps for Merchant Center typically cost a few dollars per month. The fee is considered worth it for reliable, automatically updated feeds. [L732.014]
- Software prices for product feed tools can often be negotiated, so ask the vendor before accepting list pricing. [L454.044]
- Compare tools on pricing model and features rather than picking one only by reputation. [L454.044, L454.026]
- Feed apps for Google, Bing and Facebook are sold separately because each platform has slightly different feed requirements. [L762.010]
- A feed management app that communicates with Merchant Center can find more errors than Google's diagnostics alone, and it tells you exactly which field to fix. Using such an app speeds up error correction and improves product visibility in Google Shopping. [L732.022]

**Native Shopify Google channel versus dedicated app (sources differ)**

- Older advice (2022): Google is taking over the native channel; a feed app provides better control. [L272.016]
- Older advice (2021-22): Shopify's native Google sales channel misses some useful feed data points and is harder to edit when feed changes are needed. Switch to a dedicated feed app instead. [L1170.026]
- The free Google Shopping app published by Shopify has few customization options and a 3.6-star rating in the Shopify App Store. It only makes sense for a quick start and is not suited to growing a Google Shopping business. [L1165.002]
- The free Google Merchant Center account connects directly to Shopify through the Google sales channel, following a setup process similar to the Facebook sales channel. [L1251.026]
- Use the feed app's submission path, not the Google sales channel's Merchant Center listing option. [L835.133]
- Avoid the old native Shopify Google feed plugin, which had poor reviews. It only worked for US currency, failed for customers in Canada, submitted the highest shipping cost instead of the lowest, and was poorly maintained. [L732.006]

**Naming and look-alike apps**

- Many apps in the Shopify App Store have nearly identical names, such as Google Shopping. Check the developer before installing, because the Shopify-made app and third-party apps can look the same in search results. [L1165.003]
- The sources spell the feed vendor in several ways (Simprosys, Someprocess, Some Process, Symprosis). Simprosys InfoMedia is named as the maker of "Feeds for Google Shopping". [L731.020, L272.016, L255.056, L732.007]
- "Feed for Google Shopping" (not made by Shopify) is the paid app most sources recommend. [L1165.005, L1165.007]

**Prices and trials**

| Tool | Price stated | Trial | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Feed for Google Shopping (paid third-party app) | $4.99/month up to 500 products; $8.99/month up to 1,000 products; higher above that | 21 days | [L1165.005] |
| Feed for Google Shopping (2020 recording) | About $5/month | 21 days | [L1277.065] |
| Feeds for Google Shopping (Simprosys InfoMedia, 2021-22 recording, older) | About $5/month after trial (price at time of recording) | 22 days (older) | [L731.030] |
| Some Process | $4.99/month up to 500 products | 21 days | [L255.057] |
| Symprosis Google Shopping Feed | $4.99/month | Free trial to start | [L1168.001] |
| Symprosis (other source) | Small monthly fee | Not stated | [L732.007] |
| Google Shopping Feed (feed tool) | $5/month | Not stated | [L1033.009] |
| Google Shopping Feed app (Source C) | Around $3 to $4/month, or free | Not stated | [L788.025] |
| Go Data Feed | Around $60-70/month for three feeds, more above that | Not stated | [L575.007] |
| Dedicated feed services (Data Feed Watch, Feedonomics) | From about 50 USD/month up to several hundred USD/month (older, 2021-22) | Not stated | [L1170.025] |
| Google Merchant Center default feed | Free | Not applicable | [L575.007] |

- Shopify feed apps are a cheaper option than dedicated feed services for most stores. [L1170.024, L1170.025]
- Feed for Google Shopping handles feed functions that paid feed services offer at a fraction of their cost. [L1170.024]

**Other feed tools named in the sources**

- Feed tools named as product feed apps for the Shopify store and Google Merchant Center are Flexify (a free plugin with a Facebook feed focus), Data Feed Watch, Feed Optimize, Feedonomics and Go Data Feed. One popular app costs about $5 per month. Choose by pricing model and features. [L454.026]
- Common product feed tools for Shopping and social channels are Flexify (Facebook product feed), Datafeed Watch, Feedoptimise, Feedonomics and Godatafeed. The lesson treats these as largely interchangeable. [L1033.010]
- Shopping feed management tools such as Feed Optimise, DataFeedWatch and GoDataFeed are used to optimize the product feed, including titles and product data, to improve CTR. [L1031.025, L510.031]
- Go Data Feed distributes the feed to hundreds of channels such as Amazon, eBay and price comparison sites, and lets you set rules to optimize and enrich feed data. [L575.007]

**Symprosis (Some Process / Symprosis / Someprocess)**

- Symprosis is a paid Shopify app with a small monthly fee that syncs products to Google Merchant Center. It is recommended for keeping feed data current and reliable. [L732.007]
- Symprosis is the app used to correct product feed errors that Google flags in Merchant Center. It is not the place to diagnose the error first. [L1015.003]
- Symprosis is a manual control tool for Google Shopping feed management used alongside the Google & YouTube app. [L856.073]
- Some Process (or SomeProcess) is used to sync the Shopify product catalog to Google Merchant Center. [L255.056]
- Some Process shows product status (red/green), errors, and allows contacting support for issue resolution. [L274.033]
- Advanced Symprosis features (custom labels, additional attributes, feed segmentation) are designed for high revenue scaling. Start with the app's default settings and basic product category reviews; add advanced features only when they become necessary for managing large inventories or complex feed rules. [L182.010]

**Google Shopping Feed app (Source C)**

- The Google Shopping Feed app checks each product manually and submits it to Google Merchant Center, helping avoid bans and fix suspensions. It is recommended before starting Google Ads and costs around $3 to $4 per month or is free. [L788.025]

### Feed for Google Shopping (Simprosys): Setup

**What it does**

- Feeds for Google Shopping formats Shopify product data to Google Merchant Center's required format and sends it automatically. Manual spreadsheet feeds are error-prone and time-consuming, so an app is used instead. [L731.020]
- Feed for Google Shopping connects the Shopify product feed to Google Merchant Center via the API. It updates stock status, price, title, description, SKU, photos and promotions within about 20 minutes. It is preferred over the Google channel app. *(older advice, 2021)* [L962.004]
- Set to auto update, changes in Shopify flow through the content API to Merchant Center and Google Ads in near real time. *(older advice, 2021)* [L247.082]
- It offers more control than the built-in Google channel. *(older advice, 2021)* [L801.109]
- It handles the Google product feed and provides conversion tracking. It is the recommended tool for connecting Shopify to Google. *(older advice, 2020)* [L852.074]
- It publishes products to Google Merchant Center when the product availability box is checked. Use one Google Shopping app and avoid mixing duplicates. *(older advice, 2020)* [L664.075]
- Feed for Google Shopping is a paid app of about 5 USD per month, with a free 21-day trial. It connects directly to Google Merchant Center to send the product feed automatically. *(older advice, 2020)* [L1277.065]
- It is the recommended tool for managing your product feed to Google. It is more reliable and customizable than Shopify's native Google Shopping Channel. Google is taking over the native channel; the feed app provides better control. *(older advice, 2022)* [L272.016]
- A product CSV uploaded directly to Google Merchant Center does not update automatically and only holds the data from that file. An API-based feed pushes every store change to Merchant Center in near real time, which is why price and stock changes need it. *(older advice, 2021)* [L962.005]

**Installing**

Install steps (2021-22 recording, older) [L731.021]:

1. Open the Shopify App Store from the Apps section of the Shopify menu.
2. Search for "feeds for google shopping".
3. Select Feeds For Google Shopping by Simprosys InfoMedia.
4. Click Install app.

Install step from the 2026 recording [L1165.007]:

1. Install Feed for Google Shopping from the Shopify App Store (search for the name, the app is not made by Shopify) and open it under Apps.

**Connecting to Merchant Center (Simprosys / Source B, 2021-22, older)** [L731.022]

1. Log in with the Google account used to create the Merchant Center account.
2. Allow the app access to the account.
3. Select the Merchant Center ID, checking it matches the ID shown in the top right corner.
4. Confirm the account.
5. In Merchant Center, go to Growth > Manage programs and click Get started under Shopping ads.
6. Return to the app and confirm that programs have been enabled.

**Connecting to Merchant Center (2026 recording)** [L1165.007]

1. Install Feed for Google Shopping from the Shopify App Store (search for the name, the app is not made by Shopify) and open it under Apps.
2. Select the Google Merchant Center account to link. Use the same email address for the app as for the Merchant Center account, because that address links everything.
3. Click confirm your account.
4. Read the notice inside the app about required Merchant Center programs. In Google Merchant Center, enable every program you plan to use, for example dynamic remarketing.
5. Back in the app, confirm that the programs are enabled. The app then verifies the stored data.
6. Check that the connection shows your primary domain and your merchant ID.
7. Open Feed settings to configure the feed.

**Permissions and trial**

- The feed app needs permission to manage Google Ads campaigns, product listings and Google Shopping accounts. Access must be allowed during sign-in, or the app cannot link Google Ads or insert the remarketing and conversion tags. [L731.041]
- The app has a free trial of 22 days in the 2021-22 recording (older), after which it costs about $5 per month. The 2020 and 2026 recordings give a 21-day trial. [L731.030, L1277.065, L1165.005]
- The app must be activated before the trial ends, or it stops sending product data and the ads stop running. [L731.030]
- A free sign-up for additional attributes submits rich product data, including to Facebook and Instagram Shops. [L747.019]
- A get support button in the bottom right of the screen helps when setup gets stuck or a change does not go through. [L1165.022]
- Support is included. If the product feed shows as empty after import, contact Someprocess support directly from within the app; they can diagnose configuration issues quickly. *(older advice, 2022)* [L272.017]

**Initial configuration**

- After the Merchant Center connection succeeds, the app shows Quick Sync Settings for product submission. A SWITCH TO ADVANCE INTEGRATION button leads to the more detailed feed optimization settings, which are only needed for a non-standard setup. [L731.038]
- Quick Sync Settings (2021-22, older): choose All Products for which products are submitted, Default Product Title and Default Product Description, and All Variants for variant submission. Set tax to "Yes, prices include tax" if the shown prices include tax, and enter a notification email. [L731.024]
- Which products are submitted: All Products or Products from a Collection. [L747.018]
- Product Title Preference and Product Description Preference: Default or SEO. [L747.018, L402.014]
- Variant Submission Preference: First Variant Only or All Variants. [L747.018, L402.014]
- Append Variant Name to Product Title: Yes or No. [L747.018, L402.014]
- Inventory Policy: Follow or Ignore. [L747.018, L402.014]
- Product Image Option: use second image for a product with no variant. [L747.018]
- Submit Additional Images (checkbox). [L747.018, L402.014]
- Submit a Rich Product Description (checkbox, uses HTML tags). [L747.018, L402.014]
- Product Image Option and Submit Additional Images are the sync options for images. [L402.014]
- Settings menu contents: Sync Settings From Shopify, Google Shopping Settings, Google Shopping Actions, Bing Shopping Settings, Facebook Catalog Settings, XML Feed Settings and Google Local Inventory. [L747.022]
- Sync Settings From Shopify is the page that pulls Shopify field changes into the feed. [L747.022]
- Channel Submission Control: keep Google Shopping, Google Display Ads and Surface Across Google ticked. Google Display Ads enables dynamic remarketing, and Surface Across Google covers YouTube, Google Discover and Shopping Actions. [L1170.028]
- Custom Attributes in the feed app: Color, Size, Material, Pattern and COGS. They are sent to connected channels only, do not write back to Shopify, and are used only when the same attribute is not defined in Shopify. [L1170.030]

**Default settings**

- The Default Settings section sets fallback values for Default Google Product Category, Default Age Group, Default Gender, Default Product Condition and Default Product Identifier Management. These defaults apply to products that do not have their own value in Shopify, so they should be checked before the first feed submission. [L747.023]

**Product ID formats**

| Format | Example | When to use | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Global Format (default) | shopify_US_123456_987654 | Choose the global format unless one of the exceptions applies | [L731.025] |
| SKU as Product ID |, | Used when Judge.me reviews are installed | [L731.025] |
| Variant ID as Product ID |, | Needed for the Facebook product catalog feed | [L731.025] |

**Shopping Actions (only after approval)**

- After approval, Feeds for Google Shopping hosts the switch that enables Google Shopping Actions and where the additional Shopping Actions feed data is submitted. [L738.023]
- Order Sync for Google Shopping (by Simprosys) brings Shopping Actions orders from Merchant Center into Shopify. Without it, orders are visible only inside Merchant Center and no notification reaches the merchant, so install it only after approval. [L738.024]

**Symprosis connection procedure** [L732.008]

1. Install the app and choose to sync your data.
2. Enter your Merchant Center ID (a numeric ID shown in Merchant Center) and confirm the account.
3. Check the warnings the app shows, especially a missing default shipping rate for the country.
4. Wait a few minutes for the data to be processed and check that products appear in Merchant Center.

### Data Fields, Identifiers and Categories

**Fields sent to Merchant Center**

- A Shopify product feed sends these fields to Google Merchant Center: product title, description, price, image, brand, MPN (SKU), GTIN barcode, unique product ID, variant data and product type. Most of them come directly from the Shopify product record, so complete them when the product is created. [L1170.048]
- Complete product details so Google can match your products with search queries and generate impressions. *(older advice, 2021)* [L834.056]
- A product feed app should fill in as many columns as possible so Google can match products to searches better. *(older advice, 2022)* [L665.050]
- Shopping feed identifiers are set in the Google Shopping feed app with settings for brand, MPN, SKU and GTIN handling. *(2023)* [L855.112]
- Feed tools flag issues that Google Merchant Center does not, such as HTML in product descriptions or special characters like the ampersand, which can export as broken characters. Use a feed grade check to catch these problems automatically. [L454.029]

**Editing data**

- Manage Products > select product > EDIT PRODUCT changes details in the Shopify admin. SYNC FROM SHOPIFY pulls Shopify changes into the feed. Fields such as title, description, product type and vendor (brand) come from Shopify. [L1170.027]
- Feed for Google Shopping lets you modify the product data sent to Google and Facebook. Use it to send only part of a long description or to adjust fields instead of editing product pages. *(older advice, 2022)* [L963.048]
- Feed apps let you change product data before sending it to Google and Facebook. [L510.031]

**Product IDs and offer IDs**

- In Merchant Center each variant gets its own offer ID, following the pattern shopify_[country code]_[Shopify product ID]_[variant ID], for example shopify_DE_1001_2002. Use this ID to find the exact variant when checking its status or diagnosing a suspension. [L1170.049]

**Google Product Category**

- Google Product Category values in the feed must be the full Google taxonomy path with " > " between each level, for example Health & Beauty > Personal Care > Hair Care. A category that is only the last word, or a path from a different taxonomy version, does not match the Google category list. [L747.024]
- For the Facebook product feed, the Google product category uses the same taxonomy as Google Merchant Center. Search for the Google Merchant Center product category list and choose the category that best fits your product. *(older advice, 2019)* [L1068.051]

**Currency and tax**

- The product feed is submitted in the same currency as the Shopify store's default currency. To change the feed currency, change the currency in the Shopify store settings. [L402.015, L747.018]
- Tax is set in Quick Sync Settings: choose "Yes, prices include tax" if the shown prices include tax. [L731.024]

**Images**

- If a Facebook feed rejects a product for exceeding character limits, shorten what is submitted through the feed app. Do not delete the content from the product page to make it fit. *(older advice, 2022)* [L963.049]
- When a product feed app pulls the wrong image, check its settings for image selection, since many feed apps let you choose which image to submit. [L1277.066]
- In Smart SEO, enable the additional images option so that images from the Shopify media section are submitted to the Google feed; for products without variants this is enough. For products with variants, set the variant name or SKU as the alt text of each variant image in Shopify, so the images map to the correct variant in the feed. [L1097.021]

**Title and description preference**

- When syncing the Google Shopping feed app, choose the search engine optimized title and search engine optimized description options rather than the standard product title and product description. The difference was not seen as decisive, but the SEO options are used. *(older advice, 2019)* [L1068.052]

**Reviews**

- Judge.me is a review collection app used to pull reviews into Google Shopping star ratings. A minimum of 50 store reviews is needed. It is a free app integration with Shopify. *(2023)* [L274.028]

### Re-Sync, Sync Timing and Meta Fields

**How often data reaches Google**

| Method | Timing | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Content API with auto update on | Near real time | [L247.082] (older, 2021) |
| Content API via Feed for Google Shopping | Stock, price, title, description, SKU, photos and promotions within about 20 minutes | [L962.004] (older, 2021) |
| Feed pulled on a schedule | Once a day | [L967.054] (older, 2020), [L671.084] (older, 2021), [L762.011] |
| Manual CSV upload to Merchant Center | Does not update automatically | [L962.005] (older, 2021) |
| Manual Excel export | Not reliable, see below | [L671.084] |

- Feed apps update the feed about once a day with stock status, price changes, new images and new titles. Optimizations made to products on the store, such as better titles, then reach Google Ads automatically. [L762.011]
- Connect the product feed to Google Merchant Center through an app that refreshes it every 24 hours. Do not rely on manual Excel exports, since a price change that is not updated in the feed causes mismatches. *(older advice, 2021)* [L671.084]
- Depending on the settings, the feed can be pushed to campaigns manually or imported automatically, for example once every 24 hours. *(older advice, 2020)* [L967.054]
- Use the connection option that selects the Content API as the input method, rather than Google Sheets or scheduled fetch. [L470.050]
- Check the app's settings for automatic daily sync. [L972.029]

**Meta fields that do not sync on their own**

- Shopify does not notify the app when SEO title, SEO description, custom labels, age group, gender or similar fields change. These must be re-synced manually in the app. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L747.017]
- Re-syncing meta fields overwrites any meta field changes that were made inside the app, except custom titles and custom descriptions. Check the app-side values before running the re-sync, because they are replaced. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L402.011]
- Keep the browser window open while the app is syncing meta fields. Closing it can leave the sync incomplete. Keep the window open until the app shows that syncing has finished. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L402.012]
- The re-sync pop-up offers these fields as checkboxes, with a Select All option: Age Group, Custom Labels, Google Product Category, Pattern, SEO Description, Size, Size Type, Color, Gender, Material, Product Condition, SEO Title, Size System, Unit Pricing Measure and Unit Pricing Base Measure. Tick Custom Labels for custom label changes, and tick the other fields only if they were also changed in the CSV. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L402.013]

### Merchant Center: Errors, Reviews and Support

**Account basics**

- Google Merchant Center is required to list products on Google Shopping. It is separate from Google Ads, which is the account used to run the paid ads. *(older advice, 2020)* [L1111.082]
- Google Merchant Center is a free account that holds the product feed for Google Shopping and ads. [L1251.026]
- Google Merchant Center holds the product feed used for free product listings and paid Google Shopping ads. Products that are approved there can appear in free listings, which start giving data within days. *(older advice, 2021)* [L968.050]
- Google Merchant Center holds product data for Google Shopping. Custom labels in the feed can be used to group products so that a campaign can be bulk edited. [L966.069]
- Google Merchant Center is required to upload a product feed to Google Shopping. Check for warnings, errors, and GTIN/product identifier issues. *(2023)* [L274.034]
- Google Shopping (formerly Product Listing Ads) is described as the highest-quality, cheapest traffic for e-commerce, and every store should run it. Its main threats are the product feed going offline, prices being disapproved, or poor data quality. [L575.004]
- Promotion setting: the Feed for Google Shopping promotion setting applies only to Google Shopping campaigns. It does not carry promotions over to Facebook or other connected accounts, so those must be set up separately. *(older advice, 2021)* [L962.151]

**Error panel and checks**

- The Feed for Google Shopping product list has tabs for Submitted, Submitted with Warnings and Excluded. Above them is a panel titled Errors from Merchant Center with a Refresh Product Errors button, and it shows when the error data was last refreshed, so refresh it before acting on an older error list. [L747.021]
- The dashboard's Google Status panel lists errors from Merchant Center, with a "Refresh Product Errors" button and a timestamp showing when the list was last refreshed. Before acting on the list, check that timestamp, because an old refresh can show errors that no longer exist. [L1170.053]
- Check Google Merchant Center for product feed errors (corrupt data or products not being sent) when Google Ads shows no clicks; the product feed is the source of all Shopping ads. *(older advice, 2019)* [L357.055]
- Some Process shows product status (red/green), errors, and allows contacting support for issue resolution. *(2023)* [L274.033]
- The app's shipping and store checks are a further source of errors. Check the warnings the app shows, especially a missing default shipping rate for the country. [L732.008]
- Symprosis is the app used to correct product feed errors that Google flags in Merchant Center. It is not the place to diagnose the error first. [L1015.003]
- GMCDoctors is a service for fixing Google Merchant Center product feed and account issues, such as disapprovals. *(2024)* [L1105.010]

**Review times**

- Google Merchant Center product review usually takes 1 to 3 days from pending to approved, and the first review after a product submission is short. Products can show as needs review while the check is running. *(2023)* [L1114.039]

**Contacting Google Merchant Center support (live chat)** *(2023)* [L1114.034]

1. Scroll to the bottom of Google's Merchant Center help page.
2. Click Contact us, marked with the headset icon.
3. Choose live chat rather than phone.
4. Let the agent review the store while you explain the error.

**Publishing and linking**

- Products are published to Merchant Center when the product availability box is checked. *(older advice, 2020)* [L664.075]
- The Google Shopping Feed app is used to submit product identifiers, with settings for brand, MPN, SKU and GTIN handling. *(2023)* [L855.112]
- Use the feed app's submission path, not the Google sales channel's Merchant Center listing option. [L835.133]

### Custom Labels

- Google Shopping allows five custom label slots per product. These labels are invisible to customers and exist only in the product feed data submitted to Google. [L224.003]
- Custom labels added in Shopify require approximately four hours or longer to appear in Google Ads after applying bulk edits. [L224.004]
- Custom labels can be added either in the Feed for Google Shopping app or through the Shopify CSV bulk upload. The CSV method is the quicker option for many products, because the Shopify CSV template already includes the Google custom label fields. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L747.016]
- The product detail page has a Product Labels section with Custom Label 0 to Custom Label 4, Ads Label, Ads Grouping, Shipping Label and Tax Category. Custom Labels are used to group products for campaign structure, and Shipping Label links a product to a shipping rate created in Merchant Center. Ads Label and Ads Grouping have no real function in current Google Ads. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1170.051]
- Custom labels in the feed can be used to group products so that a campaign can be bulk edited. [L966.069]

### Google & YouTube App and Merchant Center Connection

- In the Google & YouTube app on Shopify, connect only your Google Analytics 4 property. If the feed app already manages the Merchant Center connection, the Merchant Center connection should not be linked inside the Google & YouTube app. *(2023)* [L470.049]
- The Google & YouTube app should be used only to connect the Shopify store to Google Analytics. Product feed management is handled separately through a dedicated feed app. *(2025)* [L972.028]
- Symprosis is a manual control tool for Google Shopping feed management used alongside the Google & YouTube app. *(2024)* [L856.073] (Note: this describes Symprosis as used alongside the app, while L972.028 says the app is only for Google Analytics.)
- A product feed management app connected to Shopify is used to submit products to Google Merchant Center and to manage the tracking tags. *(2025)* [L472.040]
- To disconnect Merchant Center from the Google & YouTube app *(2025)* [L972.027]:
  1. Open the app in Shopify.
  2. Find Merchant Center under Connected services.
  3. Click the three dots, then click Manage settings.
  4. Disconnect it. Keep the Google Analytics connection active, since it is used for sales tracking.
- A dedicated product feed app gives more control over the feed, such as including or excluding certain products, than the default Google feed. [L972.029]

### Bing, Microsoft and Facebook Feeds

**Bing Shopping**

- Shopify apps can generate and sync a Bing shopping feed from inside the store. The workflow matches the Google feed setup, so the same app and steps can be reused. [L866.008]
- Bing Shopping Settings is a separate page in the feed app's Settings menu. [L747.022]

**Microsoft Advertising import**

1. Install and confirm the Microsoft connection in the Shopify feed app. [L1036.013]
2. Check that the connected state shows the Microsoft Advertising email, the Merchant Center account, the product catalog name, and the primary domain. [L1036.013]
3. The connection status should read connected and turned on, and the charge should read free. [L1036.013]
4. Verify the store's domain for Microsoft Merchant Center with Bing Webmaster Tools. Set it up with the store's destination URL before the validation step can be completed. [L1037.003]

**Facebook and Instagram**

- Feed tools can export one product feed to Google Shopping, Facebook Shop, Instagram product posts, Pinterest buyable pins, eBay, Bing, Amazon and affiliate networks such as ShareASale. They also handle currency conversion and multi-country export. Amazon is only an option where the supplier allows dropshipping there. [L454.030]
- Feed tools can use one feed for several channels, including Instagram product posts that show products inside the post, Pinterest buyable pins, and feeds for other countries, Google Shopping, Bing and eBay. They can also support product and seller ratings. [L1033.012]
- Shopping feed workflow: send the same optimized feed to Facebook for dynamic product ads. [L510.032]
- The Facebook Catalog Settings page is in the feed app's Settings menu. [L747.022]
- Variant ID as Product ID is needed for the Facebook product catalog feed. [L731.025]
- If a Facebook feed rejects a product for exceeding character limits, shorten what is submitted through the feed app. *(older advice, 2022)* [L963.049]
- Separate apps exist for Google, Bing and Facebook because each platform has slightly different feed requirements. [L762.010]

**TikTok**

- A built-in TikTok shop feed was offered as a theme feature to replace a paid tool. The speaker claimed that the paid tool costs $10,000 a year, a figure that was not verified during the lesson. [L709.042]

### Feed Optimization for CTR and Competitor Monitoring

**Optimization process** [L510.032]

1. Take product data from Shopify.
2. Modify the fields that influence CTR, such as images, titles, descriptions and review data.
3. Send the optimized feed to Google Shopping.
4. Send the same feed to Facebook for dynamic product ads.

- A product feed tool should be used to optimize product titles, which increases traffic and makes data analysis and optimization faster. Feeds can also be distributed to second and third tier shopping sites to improve return on ad spend. [L1033.011]
- Feed tools can pool data from Google Ads and Bing Ads to show a combined CTR. [L510.031]
- Feed tools can scan competitor product pages and search results to detect MAP pricing violations and alert you. They can also help manage product ratings and seller ratings. The manual alternative is to have an assistant check for violations. [L454.032]
- Competitor pricing alerts from feed tools notify the seller about possible MAP (minimum advertised price) violations. This helps monitor pricing without manual checks. [L1033.013]
- Feed apps update the feed about once a day with stock status, price changes, new images and new titles. Optimizations made to products on the store, such as better titles, then reach Google Ads automatically. [L762.011]

#### Further details

- Advanced Symprosis features-custom labels, additional attributes, and feed segmentation-are designed for high revenue scaling scenarios. Start with the app's default settings and basic product category reviews; add advanced features only when they become necessary for managing large inventories or complex feed rules. [L0182.010]

- Google Shopping allows five custom label slots per product. These labels are invisible to customers and exist only in the product feed data submitted to Google. [L0224.003]

- Custom labels added in Shopify require approximately four hours or longer to appear in Google Ads after applying bulk edits. [L0224.004]

- The Feed for Google Shopping app sends product data from Shopify to Google Merchant Center. Set to auto update, changes in Shopify flow through the content API to Merchant Center and Google Ads in near real time. [L0247.082]

- Some Process (or SomeProcess) is used to sync Shopify product catalog to Google Merchant Center. [L0255.056]

- Some Process pricing: $4.99 per month for up to 500 products; 21-day free trial available. [L0255.057]

- Feed for Google Shopping app (by Someprocess) is the recommended tool for managing your product feed to Google. More reliable and customizable than Shopify's native Google Shopping Channel. Google is taking over the native channel; feed app provides better control. [L0272.016]

- Someprocess's Feed for Google Shopping app includes support access. If product feed shows as empty after import, contact Someprocess support directly from within the app; they can diagnose configuration issues quickly. [L0272.017]

- Judge.me: review collection app used to pull reviews into Google Shopping star ratings. Minimum 50 store reviews needed. Free app integration with Shopify. [L0274.028]

- Some Process: Google Shopping feed management app. Shows product status (red/green), errors, and allows contacting support for issue resolution. [L0274.033]

- Google Merchant Center (GMC): required to upload product feed to Google Shopping. Check for warnings, errors, and GTIN/product identifier issues. [L0274.034]

- Google Merchant Center is checked for product feed errors (corrupt data or products not being sent) when Google Ads shows no clicks; the product feed is the source of all Shopping ads. [L0357.055]

- Re-syncing meta fields in the Feed for Google Shopping app overwrites any meta field changes that were made inside the app, except custom titles and custom descriptions. Check the app-side values before running the re-sync, because they are replaced. [L0402.011]

- Closing the browser window while the feed app is syncing meta fields can leave the sync incomplete. Keep the window open until the app shows that syncing has finished. [L0402.012]

- The re-sync pop-up in the Feed for Google Shopping app offers these fields as checkboxes, with a Select All option: Age Group, Custom Labels, Google Product Category, Pattern, SEO Description, Size, Size Type, Color, Gender, Material, Product Condition, SEO Title, Size System, Unit Pricing Measure and Unit Pricing Base Measure. Tick Custom Labels for custom label changes, and tick the other fields only if they were also changed in the CSV. [L0402.013]
